Title : William Guindon — Activist | Grande Tourbière de Blainville

# William Guindon
Activist committed to the protection of ecosystems and the rights of future generations. At the age
of 15, he brought the historical file of the Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat
Bog) before the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE SEM-26-003) and international bodies
to demand transparency and defend our common future.
> “Even if the factory is compliant or not compliant, I just want the truth. That's my goal. » —
> William Guindon, TVBL interview ↗

## File CCE SEM-26-003 & William Guindon

William Guindon (15 years old) is the author of the international citizen communication SEM-26-003
filed with the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico
Agreement)). This approach aims to enforce environmental protection laws and block the expansion of
the Stablex hazardous waste landfill site in the heart of Grande Tourbière de Blainville.

#### International procedure in progress
Following the official response from the Government of Canada (Oct. 16, 2026) and the author's
comments (Oct. 17, 2026), the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) Secretariat is
considering the recommendation for an independent factual record.

#### Major environmental risk
Documented alerts of cadmium contamination and leaching reaching up to 320× standards, threatening
the peatland and surrounding drinking water sources.

#### Policies, Ethics & Anti-SLAPP
100% independent citizen approach, protection against SLAPP lawsuits (anti-SLAPP), zero corporate
funding and strict evidentiary rigor.

## Key actions

### Highlights: from Blainville to the United Nations
- September 25, 2026 Official confirmation of CCE: First minor in the history of the SEM process
-   (since 1994) The Submissions Unit for Execution Issues (SEM) of the Commission de coopération
-   environnementale Secretariat formally confirms in writing: “since 1994, this is the first time
-   that an author has indicated to us that they are a minor at the time of submitting a file to the
-   CCE", attesting to the unprecedented historical nature of the citizen approach led by William
-   Guindon.
- August 17, 2026 Positive determination of CCE — Canada's response required by October 16, 2026 CCE
-   Secretariat makes its official determination (PDF) ↗ under sections 24.27(2) and (3) of ACEUM,
-   finding that the communication met the criteria and requesting a response from the Government of
-   Canada by October 16, 2026. William Guindon makes history by becoming the first minor to file and
-   successfully obtain a positive decision in the global registry of SEM Submissions.
- November 28, 2021 Among the first children vaccinated against COVID-19 in Quebec At the age of ten,
-   William Guindon is one of the very first Quebec children in the 5 to 11 year old age group to
-   receive the vaccine against COVID-19. His approach earned him public congratulations from the
-   Prime Minister of Quebec, François Legault, reported by the Journal de Montréal.
- April 8, 2025 Open letter in Le Devoir: “François Legault, you are destroying our future” He
-   publishes an open letter in the daily Le Devoir, in which he directly challenges the head of the
-   Quebec government on the environmental and intergenerational consequences of the Stablex site
-   expansion project, authorized by Projet de loi 93 adopted under gag order despite the unfavorable
-   opinion of BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings).
- May 1, 2026 International communication SEM-26-003 (CCE / ACEUM) He officially files communication
-   SEM-26-003 “Landfilling of hazardous materials in Blainville” with the Commission de coopération
-   environnementale, a trinational organization created within the framework of the Canada–United
-   States–Mexico Agreement. He argues that Canada is failing in its legal obligations to protect
-   migratory birds, species at risk and water quality. The CCE acknowledges receipt on May 4, 2026
-   and begins its review under sections 24.27(2) and (3) of the ACEUM.
- May 2026 Formal deposition to the United Nations Special Rapporteur He transmits to Dr. Marcos A.
-   Orellana, UN Special Rapporteur on toxic substances and human rights, the brief “Formal Deposition
-   and Urgent Appeal: Human Rights Violations and Denial of Justice – The Stablex Case and the
-   Imposition of Bill 93 in Quebec” ↗, on behalf of the Blainville community and as a representative
-   of future generations. The document argues that Projet de loi 93 violates Article 7 of the
-   Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms, Article 3 of the Universal Declaration of Human Rights
-   and UN General Assembly Resolution 76/300 recognizing the right to a healthy environment.
- June 2026 Environmental Petition No. 546 (Auditor General of Canada / CESD) Formally submits
-   Environmental Petition No. 15-546 ↗ to the Commissioner of the Environment and Sustainable
-   Development (Office of the Auditor General of Canada), triggering the legal obligation for federal
-   departments (ECCC, DFO, Health Canada) to respond formally on compliance with federal laws
-   (Fisheries Act, SARA, LCOM) in Blainville.

## The Stablex file

### A thousand-year-old ecosystem facing the landfill of hazardous waste
The Grande Tourbière de Blainville is a thousand-year-old ecosystem located in Blainville, included
by the Quebec Ministry of the Environment in the Atlas of Territories of Interest for Conservation
in the St. Lawrence Lowlands. The Stablex site, in operation since 1983, is the only center
authorized in Quebec for the final burial of materials resulting from the treatment by stabilization
and solidification of inorganic industrial residues.
- September 2023: The Bureau d'audiences publiques sur l'environnement (BAPE) concludes that the
-   expansion project (Cell 6) is “premature”.
- December 2023: A citizen sample reveals a concentration of cadmium measured at 0.016 mg/L in nearby
-   surface waters, up to 320 times the water quality criterion for the protection of aquatic life
-   (acute toxicity, MELCCFP (Québec Ministry of the Environment, the Fight against Climate Change,
-   Wildlife and Parks)).
- March 2025: The Quebec government adopts Projet de loi 93 under gag order, forcing the transfer of
-   land belonging to Ville de Blainville (City of Blainville), including 278,000 m² of wetlands, to
-   allow the expansion of the site.
- Blocked appeals: The law contains clauses excluding any legal appeal on the merits of the decision,
-   which the Centre québécois du droit de l'environnement (CQDE) describes as a serious violation of
-   the right to an effective remedy.
- Imports: Approximately 41% of the materials buried in Blainville come from outside Quebec, including
-   approximately 29% from the United States, which establishes the cross-border dimension of the
-   appeal filed with the CCE.

It is in this context that William Guindon chooses the path of law: rather than confrontation, he
mobilizes national and international mechanisms: BAPE, ACEUM, UN, to demand that Canada and Quebec
respect their own environmental obligations.

Access all the key documents submitted to the Commission de coopération environnementale register
and to international bodies.

#### Author's comments on Canada's response (October 17, 2026)
Formal brief in 7 legal grounds refuting Canada's attempt to reject it (petition BVG 0546) and
demanding the creation of an independent factual file. Accompanied by the official response from
Ottawa (72-RSP) and the formal acknowledgment of the CCE.

#### Official determination CCE & Citizen submission
Formal decision by the Secretariat CCE summoning the Government of Canada to respond to allegations
of failure to apply environmental laws, following the submission of submission SEM-26-003 by William
Guindon.

#### Complete official documentary register (222 evidentiary documents)
Find all citizen briefs, toxicological assessments, UN depositions, lobbying recordings and
ministerial correspondence with search engine and filters.

### Who is William Guindon?
William Guindon (born in 2011 in Quebec) is a Quebec environmental activist and student in the
Laurentians. Author of the communication SEM-26-003 filed at the age of 14, he became at 15 the
first minor in the history of Environmental Submissions (SEM) to obtain a positive determination of
admissibility before the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM). Consult his
detailed biography, the Stablex chronology or the press sheet.

### What is the SEM-26-003 file with CCE?
Communication SEM-26-003 (“Landfilling of hazardous materials in Blainville”) denounces the failure
to apply Canadian federal environmental laws (migratory birds, species at risk, cadmium discharge)
in the context of the expansion of the Stablex site into 278,000 m² of wetlands. Find the complete
chronology of the file.

### Why is Grande Tourbière de Blainville threatened?
The Grande Tourbière de Blainville is home to exceptional biodiversity and constitutes a major
carbon sink. The expansion project would allow millions of tons of tailings to be landfilled,
destroying 278,000 m² of rare ecosystems and posing risks to waters where cadmium concentrations
have been measured up to 320 times the water quality criterion for the protection of aquatic life
(acute toxicity, MELCCFP, sample Dec. 2, 2023). View the perimeters on the interactive GIS map.

### Has William Guindon received the official response from the Government of Canada?
Yes. On October 16, 2026, the Government of Canada formally filed its official response (Exhibit
72-RSP) with the CCE Secretariat. William Guindon received notification of this and filed on October
17, 2026 a memorandum of observations in 7 legal grounds refuting Ottawa's attempt to avoid and
requesting the creation of an independent factual file.

### What are the next steps following the submission of Canada's response?
Following the submission of Canada's response on October 16, 2026 and citizen submissions on October
17, 2026, the CCE Secretariat is reviewing the file under section 24.28 of ACEUM to determine
whether it recommends the development of an independent Factual File subject to a vote by the
Trinational Council of Ministers. Follow the procedure live.

# Structural adultism & Civic capacity of minors
Scientific declaration of support subscribed in Berlin on October 14, 2026 by Prof. Dr. Manfred
Liebel and Philip Meade, M.A. in support of William Guindon (SEM-26-003) and the process of Petition
e-7834 to the House of Commons of Canada.

### Statement of International Scientific Support (Berlin, October 14, 2026)
On October 14, 2026, an official declaration of scientific support was signed in Berlin by Prof. Dr.
Manfred Liebel (professor emeritus of sociology at the Technische Universität Berlin) and Philip
Meade, M.A. (Fachhochschule Potsdam / Alice Salomon Hochschule Berlin), co-authors of the reference
treatise Adultism: A Critical Introduction to Discrimination Against Children (2026).

#### Certified autograph signatures (Berlin):

## 1. Official full declaration (Bilingual)

Subscribed in Berlin on Wednesday October 14, 2026 (48 hours before the federal deadline of October
16). Switch between the certified French version, the original English text or the comparative view:

### STATEMENT OF SCIENTIFIC SUPPORT — YOUTH CIVIC CAPACITY AND PETITION e-7834
> “From the perspective of the sociology of childhood and children's rights research, civic and
> analytical capacity is not acquired by magic on the day of the 18th birthday: it is demonstrated in
> practice through concrete agency. The rigor of the communication SEM-26-003 — officially declared
> admissible by the Commission de coopération environnementale (Commission for Environmental
> Cooperation) in the determination DET-26-003 — proves that a 15-year-old citizen is fully capable of
> producing an administrative and scientific file of the highest level. Conversely, the institutional
> barriers documented by William Guindon (williamguindon.me/age.html) constitute typical examples of
> structural adultism, where authorities use minor status as a pretext to ward off rigorous citizen
> scrutiny. I therefore fully support the approach of petition e-7834 to the House of Commons of
> Canada, and in particular the establishment of a rebuttable presumption of capacity for young people
> aged 14 to 17 in administrative procedures. »
- Prof. Dr. Manfred Liebel (Emeritus Professor of Sociology at the Technische Universität Berlin,
-   Honorary Professor at the Master MACR at the University of Applied Sciences Potsdam, co-author of
-   Adultism: A Critical Introduction to Discrimination Against Children, 2026).
- Philip Meade, M.A. (Lecturer of the MACR Masters at the University of Applied Sciences Potsdam and
-   the Alice Salomon University of Berlin, co-author of Adultism: A Critical Introduction to
-   Discrimination Against Children, 2026).

### STATEMENT OF ACADEMIC SUPPORT — YOUTH CIVIC CAPACITY & PETITION e-7834
> “From the perspective of the sociology of childhood and children's rights research, civic and
> analytical capacity is not acquired magically on a person's 18th birthday; it is demonstrated in
> practice through concrete agency. The rigor of submission SEM-26-003 —officially declared admissible
> by the Commission for Environmental Cooperation in determination DET-26-003 —proves that a
> 15-year-old citizen is fully capable of producing an administrative and scientific dossier of the
> highest standard. i.e., the institutional obstacles documented by William Guindon
> (williamguindon.me/age.html) are textbook examples of structural adultism, in which authorities use
> minor status as a pretext to dismiss rigorous citizen scrutiny. I therefore fully support the
> approach of Canadian House of Commons Petition e-7834, and in particular the establishment of a
> rebuttable presumption of capacity for youth aged 14 to 17 in administrative procedures.”
- Prof. Dr. Manfred Liebel (Professor Emeritus of Sociology at Technological University of Berlin,
-   Honorary Professor at MA Childhood Studies and Children’s Rights at Potsdam University of Applied
-   Sciences, co-author of Adultism: A Critical Introduction to Discrimination Against Children,
-   2026).
- Philip Meade, MA (Lecturer at MA Childhood Studies and Children’s Rights at Potsdam University of
-   Applied Sciences and at Alice Salomon University of Applied Sciences Berlin, co-author of
-   Adultism: A Critical Introduction to Discrimination Against Children, 2026).

## 2. Conceptual insight: From ageism to structural adultism

In the media space and everyday language, any discrimination based on age is often described as
“ageism”. In contemporary sociological research on children's rights, this term is insufficient:

#### Why “ageism” is reductive
The word “ageism” refers to interpersonal stereotypes or aging. It obscures the political,
institutional and legal dimension of the subordination of young people.

#### Adultism
Defined by Liebel and Meade (2026), adultism designates a relationship of systemic domination by
which adults arrogate discretionary power over minors and systematically presume their civic
incapacity.

#### Structural adultism in administrative law
It is expressed when administrations establish the civil age as an end of inadmissibility: refusal
of access to public documents, refusal to respond on the technical merits, or infantilization during
public hearings.

#### Concrete Agency
The ability demonstrated in the field by a young citizen to investigate, analyze complex technical
data and obtain a historic decision before the CCE (ACEUM) shatters the theoretical presumption of
incompetence.

## 3. Petition e-7834 to the House of Commons of Canada

### Petition e-7834 to the House of Commons assembled in Parliament

#### Official text submitted to the House of Commons:
- Whereas section 15(1) of the Canadian Charter guarantees everyone the same protection and equal
-   benefit of the law, without discrimination based on age;
- Whereas article 12 of the Convention on the Rights of the Child, ratified by Canada, requires that
-   the opinion of the discerning child be heard and considered according to his age and maturity in
-   administrative procedures;
- Whereas the Code civil du Québec (Civil Code of Québec) allows minors aged 14 and over to consent
-   alone to certain care, under the conditions of articles 14 and 17;
- Whereas Canada currently has no federal legal framework or independent commissioner to protect young
-   people against administrative and institutional paternalism;
- Whereas automatically presuming the incapacity of young people aged 14 to 17 in their civic and
-   administrative activities contravenes their intellectual integrity and may constitute
-   discrimination.
- To adopt, within its areas of jurisdiction, a framework law against ageism and paternalism in
-   services and institutions, public or private, under federal jurisdiction, and to negotiate a
-   pan-Canadian agreement;
- To create an independent Federal Youth Commissioner (ombudsman), accessible to young people,
-   responsible for receiving complaints and investigating federal institutions;
- To establish, for young people aged 14 to 17, an automatic and rebuttable presumption of capacity to
-   participate in administrative decisions concerning them, reversible only after an individualized
-   assessment of their age, maturity and competence, never on age alone, with written reasons,
-   respect for autonomy and the right to be heard.

#### Test of the facts: Chronology of sponsorship processes (October 2026)
On the official electronic petition portal of the House of Commons (NosCommunes), a citizen petition
must be authorized by a Member of Parliament before being published and open to public signatures.
Without this formal sponsorship, the procedure remains blocked. In accordance with parliamentary
regulations, each elected official has discretionary sponsorship power. The chronology below records
in a strictly factual manner the exchanges that took place with the office of Ms. Anna Gainey,
Federal Member of Parliament for Notre-Dame-de-Grâce—Westmount and Secretary of State for Children
and Youth of the Government of Canada.

Transmission by William Guindon of an official request to the MP's parliamentary address ([email
protected]): “Electronic petition e-7834 — Fight against ageism and recognition of the capacity of
young people”. Presentation of the approach and request for sponsorship to authorize publication on
the NosCommunes portal.

Receipt of generic automatic acknowledgments of receipt from the parliamentary office, without
personalized support or response on the merits of the request.

Transmission of an urgent request indicating the procedural deadline for publication on NosCommunes
(October 16), with a certified copy to the office of the Secretary of State for Children and Youth.

Follow-up email: “Petition e-7834 blocked on NosCommunes — request for intervention from your
office”. New automatic response generated by the desktop, without background processing.

Several citizens are writing directly to the MP's parliamentary office to support William Guindon's
approach and request authorization to sponsor petition e-7834.

The office notifies its refusal to follow up on the sponsorship request. William Guindon formally
acknowledges this in writing. A personal courtesy response is sent by the office (“Thank you for
contacting us… we are always happy to receive messages from young Canadians”), confirming the
absence of sponsorship and the absence of authorization for publication on the parliamentary portal.

Despite the sending of citizen support, it was noted that emails were classified as junk mail
(“spam”) and that no sponsorship follow-up was granted by the office.

The official House of Commons petition system formally records: "Refused by Anna Gainey on October
31, 2026 at 7:18 p.m. (ET)". The Member of Parliament for Notre-Dame-de-Grâce—Westmount and
Secretary of State for Children and Youth officially declines the sponsorship required for the
publication of petition e-7834.

In accordance with the procedure of the House of Commons, the request for authorization to publish
petition e-7834 is immediately redirected and transmitted to another federal Member in order to seek
the parliamentary sponsorship necessary to open it for public signatures.

#### Democratic scope: the documentary demonstration of structural adultism
This situation concretely illustrates the thesis developed by Prof. Dr Manfred Liebel and Philip
Meade: young people aged 14 to 17, not having the right to vote, do not have any autonomous
institutional lever to submit their proposals to public debate. Even when a young citizen prepares a
documented petition consistent with section 15 of the Canadian Charter and section 12 of the
Convention on the Rights of the Child, his or her approach depends entirely on the discretionary
goodwill of an elected adult. The gap between the mission of Secretary of State for Youth and the
refusal to authorize the publication of the petition demonstrates why the formal recognition of a
presumption of civic capacity and the creation of an independent Federal Youth Commissioner are
essential.

Electronic petition e-7834 remains registered in the registry of the House of Commons and can be
sponsored by any federal MP sitting in Parliament. If you wish to support democratic opening to
14-17 year olds, you can contact your own constituency MP to ask them to sponsor and authorize the
publication of petition e-7834 on NosCommunes.ca.

In the event of a spill, toxic release, mass animal mortality or suspicious chemical odor:

## Who should I contact to report a situation?

For your report to be processed quickly and efficiently by sworn inspectors, it is essential to
choose the right level of intervention depending on the nature of the event:

### Emergency-Environment
Sudden events, ongoing chemical spills, acute toxic fumes or immediate danger to waterways.
- Leaking tanker truck or active spill
- Oil slick or chemical on water
- Persistent suffocating chemical odors

### Ministry of the Environment
Environmental violations noted, damage to wetlands or non-compliance with ministerial
authorizations.
- Unauthorized filling or drainage of bog
- Abnormal discharge of wastewater or suspicious coloring
- Illegal dumping of hazardous materials or contaminated soil
- Excavation work without certificate of authorization
- Abnormal mortality of fish or aquatic fauna

### Ville de Blainville
For problems that fall under the municipality and municipal planning regulations.
- Wild dumping of waste or bulky items
- Felling of trees or deforestation without municipal permit
- Local nuisances (noise outside of hours, dust)
- Obstructed ditches or blocked storm flow
- Cleanliness of marked public trails

## Methodical guide: How to document an observation

A well-founded report is based on verifiable, precise and reproducible facts. Follow these 8 steps
to build an impeccable file:
- Note the exact date and time: Indicate the exact start and end time of your observation.
- Note the exact location: Note the GPS coordinates (via your phone) or fixed visual landmarks
-   (terminal number, post, intersection, footbridge).
- Describe the phenomenon factually: Describe what you see, smell or hear without excessive
-   interpretation (e.g.: “Presence of an iridescent film on an area of ​​approximately 15 m²” rather
-   than “It’s polluted”).
- Take contextualized photographs: Combine a wide overview to identify locations with clear close-up
-   views.
- Record a short video: If the event involves movement (cloudy water flow, backflow, smoke emission),
-   a 15 to 30 second video provides convincing dynamic proof.
- Specify whether the event is active or completed: Clearly indicate whether the spill or work is in
-   progress at the time you submit the report.
- Strictly respect safety rules: Do not touch any product, do not walk on unstable ground and never
-   leave authorized public paths.
- Keep a dated copy of your shipment: Note the reference number of the file sent to the authorities
-   for citizen monitoring.

#### Fundamental safety & legality reminder
It is strictly forbidden to enter private property or fenced industrial sites. Never take liquid or
gas samples yourself without certified protective equipment. Your safety and respect for the law
take precedence over any observation.

## Participatory science: Identifying biodiversity on iNaturalist

Each observation of living species (sphagnum moss, salamanders, ferns, birds, insects) on authorized
public trails constitutes conclusive evidence to document the ecological richness of the Grande
Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog) and enrich the environmental file.

### Step-by-step guide: How to participate with iNaturalist
- Download the free iNaturalist app to your smartphone (links below).
- Create your free account or log in in seconds.
- Explore the authorized public trails (Boisé des lacs Fauvel, Plan Bouchard trails). Reminder:
-   Respect nature, do not leave the footbridges and do not touch vulnerable species.
- Take photos of the plants, mushrooms, birds, insects or moss you observe.
- Share your sighting: iNaturalist's artificial intelligence and global community of biologists will
-   accurately identify the species.
- Automatic addition: Your geolocated observations in the area are automatically associated with the
-   official project SEM-26-003.

## Check information & Environmental monitoring

A serious environmental file is based on rigorous information hygiene. Here is the analysis grid
applied to all the documents in the register:

### The 5 reflexes of critical verification
  ▸ Type of information
  ▸ Reliability level
  ▸ Application example
  · Verified scientific data
  · Very high
  · ISO/CEAEQ accredited analysis report, iNaturalist inventory validated by peers.
  · Official state document
  · Pupil
  · Decision CCE of August 17, 2026, certificate of authorization MELCCFP, government decree.
  · Direct personal observation
  · To be documented
  · Time-stamped photo taken from a public trail with GPS coordinates.
  · Rumor or unverified message
  · Not eligible
  · Claim on social networks without source document or probative photo.

## Don’ts: Security, legality & Protection of minors

### The 8 strict prohibitions
- Never enter private property or a fenced industrial area (violation of home/property).
- Never touch, inhale or taste any unknown substance, cloudy standing water or suspicious residue.
- Never take samples without certified equipment and compliant protocol.
- Never spread false alarms or unsubstantiated sensationalist claims.
- Never harass municipal, industrial or civil servants in the exercise of their duties.
- Do not degrade the environment to document (do not trample sphagnum moss off trail or pick rare
-   plants).
- Do not venture alone into isolated marshy areas without means of communication.
- Never publish photos of identifiable people without their prior written consent.

### Good citizen practices
- Stay on marked public trails and authorized observation decks.
- Document with composure and precision (dates, times, photos, GPS).
- Use the official forms from the Ministry and the City for traced processing.
- Respect professional secrecy and the presumption of legality until the facts are established.
- Show courtesy in all democratic questions.

### Official notice of protection for minors
If you are under 18 years old and wish to act to protect the environment: never go alone to an
observation site and do not participate in any field process without the explicit consent of your
parents or legal guardian.

Consult the official policy: Citizenship status of minor applicant & Legal protection ↗.

## Commitment to independence & financial integrity

#### Fully self-financing and absolute refusal of donations
In order to preserve absolute political, moral and legal independence (see the Declaration of
Independence and Integrity ↗), William Guindon does not solicit or accept any financial donations
for the SEM-26-003 registry, nor any partisan or corporate funding. The only remuneration he
receives is professional fees for his conferences and popular education interventions.

The scientific equipment for citizen analysis (detection strips, sterile containers, inventory
equipment) is strictly self-funded. No external collection or wishlist platforms are active on the
site.

## Frequently asked questions (Citizen FAQ)

Direct and verified answers to the most frequently asked questions about the protection of Grande
Tourbière and reporting procedures:

### What should I do if I notice suspicious drainage near a humid environment?
If the spill presents an immediate danger, a suffocating chemical odor or a large volume, contact
Urgence-Environnement immediately at 1 866 694-5454 (24/7). Take photos and videos from a safe
distance, note the exact time and GPS coordinates, without ever coming into direct contact with the
liquid.

### How do you know if a problem is the responsibility of the City or the Ministry of the Environment?
The Ville de Blainville (450 434-5200 / town planning: 450 434-5254) deals with local illegal
dumping, municipal tree felling, construction site nuisances and town planning regulations. The
Ministry of the Environment (MELCCFP) and Urgence-Environnement (1 866 694-5454) deal with toxic
discharges, the filling of wetlands, dangerous materials and violations of the Loi sur la qualité de
l'environnement (Environment Quality Act).

### Can I make an environmental report anonymously in Quebec?
Yes. The online reporting form of the Ministry of the Environment of Quebec and the
Urgence-Environnement telephone line allow reports to be transmitted confidentially or anonymously,
while receiving a file number for follow-up.

### How can I prove that a protected species lives in the bog?
Use the free iNaturalist app from permitted public trails. Take sharp photos with geotagging enabled
on your phone. The scientific community and experts validate the identification of the species
(sphagnum moss, salamanders, birds), constituting admissible and time-stamped proof.

### Why is the SEM-26-003 file handled by an international commission (CCE / ACEUM)?
The Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE), established by the Canada-United States-Mexico
Agreement (ACEUM, chapter 24), allows any citizen or NGO to submit a communication demonstrating
that a State party is failing to ensure the effective application of its environmental laws. The CCE
(Commission for Environmental Cooperation) deemed the communication SEM-26-003 admissible on August
17, 2026.

### What does Canada's response of October 16, 2026 (72-RSP) contain and what's next?
On October 16, 2026, the Government of Canada submitted its official 59-page response (72-RSP)
requesting the closure of SEM-26-003 under the pretext of the Blainville municipal appeal and
federal petition no. 0546 (BVG/CEDD). As of October 17, 2026, William Guindon sent his brief of
observations proving that no court examines the non-application of federal laws (LCOM, LEP, LCPE).
The CCE Secretariat now has 60 working days (art. 24.28 ACEUM) to recommend a factual record, while
federal ministers must respond to petition 0546 by November 10, 2026.

### Can a minor participate in environmental protection?
Yes, through citizen science (iNaturalist), learning, awareness and civic monitoring. However, any
action on the ground, public questioning or contact with the media must be supervised and authorized
by the parents or legal guardians in order to guarantee the safety and well-being of the young
person.

### How can I support William Guindon without donating money?
William Guindon applies a strict policy of independence and refuses any financial donations for the
SEM-26-003 register. You can help by sending form letters to elected officials, using iNaturalist on
public trails, or relaying verified official documents and briefs.

Title : Protection Against SLAPP Suits — William Guindon
==============================================================================

# SLAPP Protection Statement (Anti-SLAPP)
Official position of William Guindon regarding strategic prosecutions against public mobilization

## 1. Purpose and scope

This statement sets out the position of William Guindon and his site regarding SLAPP suits, i.e.
legal actions whose real purpose is not to win a case, but to intimidate, exhaust or silence a
person who participates in the public debate.

This protection applies to all actions, appeal filings (CCE SEM-26-003, UN deposition), open
letters, media interventions, field investigations and citizen mobilizations carried out by William
Guindon in favor of the protection of Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog),
the safe management of dangerous materials and the rights of future generations.

## 2. Commitment to good faith and quality of information

William and the site undertake, prior to any distribution:
- In good faith: all formulation is made in good faith, without intention of harming anyone.
- Veracity and verification: the facts stated are based on identifiable sources, public documents,
-   measurements or verified testimonies, presented with caution and without exaggeration.
- In a measured tone: comments are presented as verified factual assertions or, when they are
-   opinions, clearly identified as such (comment, open letter, opinion).
- The absence of harassment and intimidation: communications are not intended to harass, intimidate or
-   insult any person.
- Upon rectification: any demonstrated inaccuracy is corrected promptly, visibly and transparently.
- Respecting the public interest: information serves public debate and the general interest.

These commitments constitute the conduct which the site makes its standard so that its expression
remains legitimate, proportionate and defensible.

## 3. Position regarding SLAPP suits

William and his parents believe that strategic recourses aimed at muzzling public debate on issues
of general interest (environment, public health, rights of future generations) are contrary to the
public interest and citizen participation.

Consequently:
- The site will not give in to judicial intimidation in its legitimate information work;
- Any threat of prosecution will be treated seriously and calmly, with the help of a lawyer, and with
-   the agreement and presence of William's parents;
- The site will rely, where applicable, on the protections recognized in Quebec against abusive
-   prosecutions, in particular the provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure relating to abusive
-   requests and “public mobilization” (art. 51 et seq.);
- The site will maintain complete documentation of its publications, sources and evidence in order to
-   be able to demonstrate the good faith and seriousness of its work.

## 4. Recognition of the dissuasive cost of SLAPP suits

William is a minor and a student, without financial resources to support a long legal procedure. A
SLAPP suit, even if unfounded, can create a disproportionate deterrent effect. It is precisely for
this reason that the site:
- favors verifiable factual assertions, based on public documents and transparent methods;
- limits his comments to what is necessary for information of public interest;
- invites any person concerned to exercise a right of reply and to request a correction before any
-   appeal;
- documents each step rigorously.

## 5. Right of reply and amicable resolution

The site undertakes to examine in good faith, quickly and graciously, any request for rectification
or well-founded right of reply, presented in writing. Amicable resolution is always preferred.

## 6. Role of parents (protection)

William being a minor, any legal communication, any threat of prosecution, any official request or
any settlement proposal is transmitted to his parents or guardian, who monitor it with the help of a
lawyer if necessary. No one should contact William directly with a legal question without his
parents being informed.

## 7. Declaration status

This statement expresses a position and editorial commitments of williamguindon.me. It is published
as a stand-alone policy, with no signature required, for information and transparency. It does not
constitute legal advice and does not bind third parties. Any legal question must be entrusted to a
lawyer from the Barreau du Québec.

PAGE 6/50 · https://williamguindon.me/apercu.html
Title : File SEM-26-003 Blainville Explained — William Guindon
==============================================================================

How to read this document. It is designed so that everyone understands in depth the legal and
scientific scope of the case, without unnecessary jargon. Each technical term is explained, and each
time you come across the words En clair, this is the translation into current French of the point of
law or environmental report.

Scope and rigor of the procedure: The Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE) formally
deemed the communication admissible following extensive screening under Articles 24.27(1), (2) and
(3) of the ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement). This historic determination triggers the
legal obligation for the Government of Canada to provide a detailed response on the application of
federal environmental laws (Fisheries Act, Species at Risk Act, Migratory Birds Convention Act,
CEPA).

Methodological note & legal framework: Informative summary resulting from citizen analysis of public
documents. Consult the methodological note, the good faith framework and the Anti-SLAPP declaration
↓ .

## Part 01 The story, told simply

In Blainville, in the Laurentians north of Montreal, there is a large swamp called Grande Tourbière
(Great Peat Bog). A bog is a spongy wetland where dead vegetation accumulates without completely
rotting. These environments filter water, store carbon and shelter species that are found nowhere
else.

Next door is a company called Stablex. Its job: to receive dangerous industrial waste – sludge
filled with heavy metals, for example – to mix it with cement to “freeze” it, then to bury it in
large airtight pits called cells. Its first five cells reach saturation. He needs a sixth.

The land coveted for this cell no. 6 belonged to the Ville de Blainville (City of Blainville), which
refused to cede it. In March 2025, the Quebec government resolved the problem in an expeditious
manner: it passed a special law, Loi 93 (Bill 93), passed overnight under a gag order (an emergency
procedure that cuts short the debate). This law expropriates the City, pays it $17 million,
transfers the land to Stablex — and in the process forecloses most recourses before the courts. The
City and the Metropolitan Community of Montreal tried to challenge it: they lost in the Superior
Court on April 2, 2025, then in the Court of Appeal on April 16, 2025.

The machines have arrived. The trees have fallen. It was at this moment that William Guindon, a
citizen of Blainville then aged 14 at the start of his efforts (now 15 years old), opened an
unprecedented legal front: rather than attacking Quebec or Stablex – two avenues blocked by the Loi
93 – he questioned the direct responsibility of Ottawa.

His legal reasoning is implacable:

Quebec can legislate on the transfer of land, but there are Canadian federal laws that protect fish
habitat, migratory birds and species at risk across the country, including on private or municipal
land. These laws fall under the exclusive jurisdiction of Ottawa. And Ottawa remained completely
silent.

The strength of the case lies in this demonstration: it does not criticize the federal government
for having poorly exercised its discretionary power, but for never having exercised it. No known
federal inspections, no permits issued or refused under the Fisheries Act or the Species at Risk
Act, no reasoned decisions.

By mobilizing the international mechanism of chapter 24 of the ACEUM (SEM process) from May 1, 2026,
then by filing on July 16, 2026 a consolidated communication of exceptional density - 15-page brief,
102-page analytical annex and 222 evidentiary documents -, he has built a file that stands the test
of international filtering.

On August 17, 2026, the Commission de coopération environnementale (Commission for Environmental
Cooperation) made a historic determination: the communication met all of the admissibility criteria
of the North American treaty. The Government of Canada is legally required to respond by October 16,
2026.

## Part 02 Who's who

Environmental activist and citizen researcher residing in Blainville, born in 2011 (official name in
the register: William Tristan Logan Théo Guindon). At the age of 15, he became the first minor
author in the history of the SEM mechanism (since 1994) to successfully cross the threshold of
international admissibility for citizen communication. Acting voluntarily and autonomously — without
a legal firm or partisan funding — he assembled a body of evidence of 222 documents to preserve
Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog) and defend the environmental rights of
future generations.

It is he, and he alone, who is in question. Two ministries are involved: Environment and Climate
Change Canada (ECCC), which enforces laws on migratory birds, species at risk and toxic substances;
and Fisheries and Oceans Canada (DFO), which administers the Fisheries Act. At the time of the
events, the Minister of the Environment was Julie Dabrusin.

Operator of the hazardous waste landfill site. It is a subsidiary of two American companies:
Republic Services and US Ecology. Crucial point: Stablex is not being sued here. The SEM mechanism
never targets a company, only a State. She has no formal right of reply in this procedure.

Quebec adopted Loi 93, issued authorization decree 515-2025, conducted provincial inspections. But
the SEM process is only aimed at central governments. The Quebec documents only serve as proof of
the facts here: they demonstrate that work, rejections and overruns exist.

Commission de coopération environnementale, created in 1994, is headquartered at 1001
Robert-Bourassa Boulevard in Montreal. It has three bodies: the Council (the Ministers of the
Environment of the three countries - it is he who votes), the Secretariat (the permanent
administration, which investigates complaints) and the Joint Public Consultative Committee (of
citizens of the three countries, consultative role).

Director of Legal Affairs and Communications on Enforcement Issues at the Secretariat. The decision
is copied to the alternate representatives of the three countries (Michael Bonser for Canada, Camila
Isabel Zepeda Lizama for Mexico, Usha-Maria Turner for the United States) and to the executive
director Jorge Daniel Taillant.

## Part 03 The place: three superimposed layers

### 3.1 — The Grande Tourbière de Blainville
A wetland complex which, according to provincial inspection reports on file, includes “a marsh, a
swamp, shallow water and a peat bog”. There we find plants indicative of wetlands — pitcher plant (a
carnivorous plant), cotton grass — growing on permanent sphagnum mats. The Bureau d'audiences
publiques sur l'environnement (BAPE), in its report No. 371, highlighted the “exceptional ecological
value” of the sector and advised the government against authorizing the project.

A geological detail that matters enormously. The area is underlain by thick deposits of clay from
the Champlain Sea — a sea that covered the St. Lawrence Valley about 10,000 years ago. These clays,
15 meters thick on the surface and up to 50 meters deep, are subject to “deep ravinement”: the water
creates tree-shaped networks. In practical terms, this means that any sediment or contaminant that
reaches the ground is transported quickly and far to waterways. It is not a ground that holds back;
it is a ground that leads.

### 3.2 — Cell No. 6
The land in question is lot 6 695 053 of the Quebec land register. According to media coverage and
BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings), it includes approximately 9 hectares of
wetlands and 58 hectares of woodlands. According to the proponent's impact study, the 52-hectare
study area contains 29.2 hectares of wetlands (56% of the surface area), including a 19.1-hectare
tree swamp. The planned backfilling would cover 278,000 m², or 27.8 hectares. (These different
figures relate to different perimeters — see Part 9.)

The agreement in principle signed on March 11, 2020 between the City and the developer provides for
24 hours a day, 7 days a week operation. The new site would allow Stablex to operate for around
forty more years, until around 2065.

### 3.3 — What was there before: Camp Bouchard
This is perhaps the most surprising element of the file. From 1942 to 1980, this same land was home
to Camp Bouchard, a munitions production plant and explosives depot operated by the Canadian
Department of National Defense. In other words: the soil was first contaminated by the federal
government itself.

The site still appears on the Federal Contaminated Sites Inventory (number 06875001), maintained by
the Treasury Board Secretariat. According to the data cited in the complaint:
- Identified contaminants: metals, metalloids and organometallics in groundwater and soil
- File status: “Active” — it is not closed
- Management strategy: “Additional assessment” — work is not completed
- Expenditures in 2025-2026: $0 on evaluation, $0 on restoration, $0 on monitoring
- Area restored in 2025-2026: 0 hectare
- Historical total expenditures: approximately $147,000 for a 4-hectare military site operated for 38
-   years

Stablex itself publicly attributes part of the metals detected to this previous military use. The
author draws two formidable questions from this: (1) did the Ministry of Defense decontaminate
before ceding the land? A freedom of information request reportedly found no documentation of
decontamination work. (2) If the metals found in the stream come in part from the former federal
military site, why has Ottawa never investigated?

## Part 04 The mechanism, from A to Z

### 4.1 — Where does this tool come from
In 1994, when signing NAFTA, the three countries feared that one state would be tempted to attract
factories by turning a blind eye to pollution. They therefore created, in parallel, an environmental
agreement equipped with a citizen valve. When NAFTA was replaced by ACEUM on July 1, 2020, this
valve was retained and written directly into the trade treaty, in articles 24.27 and 24.28.

Curiosity of vocabulary: the official French text of ACEUM speaks of “observations on questions of
application”. The Commission continues to use the word “communications”. Both mean exactly the same
thing: a citizen complaint. The acronym SEM comes from the English Submissions on Enforcement
Matters.

### 4.2 — First question: what is an “environmental law”?
All the legal sorting of the decision follows from this definition. Here is the official text, in
article 24.1 of ACEUM:
> “environmental law means a law or regulation of a Party, or one of its provisions […] the primary
> object of which is the protection of the environment, or the prevention of a danger to health or
> human life, by, as the case may be: a) the prevention, reduction or control of the discharge […] of
> pollutants or contaminants; (b) the control of dangerous or toxic chemicals, substances, materials
> or waste […]; (c) the protection or conservation of wild flora and fauna, including endangered
> species, their habitat and specially protected natural areas […]” “law or regulation means […] for
> Canada, an Act of the Parliament of Canada or a regulation made under an Act of the Parliament of
> Canada which may be enforced by action of the central government. »

For an article of law to be retained by the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation), it must
meet three strict conditions: (1) have as its primary purpose the protection of the environment or
human health; (2) be a federal law or regulation passed in Ottawa; (3) fall under the application of
the federal government. It is this surgical filter that allowed the Secretariat to focus the
investigation on the four federal ecological pillars invoked in the communication (Fisheries Act,
SARA, LCOM, CEPA), while ruling out provisions of a purely commercial or general constitutional
nature.

### 4.3 — The five formal criteria (article 24.27(2))
First filter. The complaint must satisfy all five — not three out of five, not “overall.” Official
text on the left, translation on the right, verdict in this case.
  ▸ Text of ACEUM
  ▸ In plain language
  ▸ Verdict
  · has)
  · “are made in writing, in French, English or Spanish”
  · Write, in one of the three official languages ​​of the continent.
  · ✔ Validated (initial exam on June 3)
  · b)
  · “clearly identify the person presenting them”
  · Sign. No anonymous complaints.
  · c)
  · “give sufficient information to allow consideration of the observations, including documentary evidence […] and indication of which environmental law may not have been applied”
  · Provide real evidence, and say precisely which article of which law is not applied. This is the most demanding criterion.
  · ✔ Validated (consolidation of August 17)
  · d)
  · “appear to be aimed at promoting enforcement rather than harassing the industry”
  · Do not use the mechanism as a commercial weapon against a competitor.
  · e)
  · “indicate whether the matter has been communicated in writing to the competent authorities of the Party, and, if so, the response of the Party”
  · Have first warned the government in writing, and given it a chance to act. You don't run internationally without knocking on Ottawa's door.

### 4.4 — The four criteria of opportunity (article 24.27(3))
Second filter: does the complaint merit disturbing a government?
  · “if it is alleged that harm has been suffered by the person making the observations”
  · Be personally affected. The author invokes environmental damage (his living environment), health damage (exposure) and intergenerational damage (he will inherit the site).
  · “if the observations […] raise questions for which in-depth study would be conducive to achieving the objectives of this chapter”
  · Would digging into the subject do anything useful for North America?
  · “if the private remedies provided for by the law of the Party have been exercised”
  · Have first tried the remedies available at home — or credibly explain why it was impossible.
  · “if the observations are not drawn exclusively from information from mass media”
  · Don't settle for a pile of newspaper clippings. We need official documents, studies, data.

An important counterbalance, that said: the Secretariat also recalls that these criteria “must not
constitute an insurmountable procedural obstacle” and must be interpreted in the broad sense. He
cites three precedents in this sense (Loggerhead Turtle 2021, Fairview Terminal 2021, Gulf of
California Porpoise 2021). The door is narrow, but we must not barricade it.

### 4.5 — Deadlines, calculated to the nearest day
The Secretariat explains in a footnote how it counts: in calendar days; the day of departure is not
counted, the day of arrival is; if the deadline falls on a Saturday, Sunday or public holiday, it
slides to the next working day; a document received on a non-working day is deemed received on the
following working day.

### 4.6 — The complete journey, step by step
To compile the factual record, the Secretariat may draw on any public information, contributions
from interested persons, opinions of independent experts and the work of the Joint Public Advisory
Committee. This is where the file could become a real field investigation.

### 4.7 — The nature of the SEM mechanism: the power of the international investigation
- Force the federal government to come out of its silence by submitting a written, official and public
-   response on the application of its laws.
- Present the facts on a North American scale before the Council of Ministers of the Environment of
-   Canada, the United States and Mexico.
- Commission an independent investigation (the factual record) involving scientific experts,
-   interviews with authorities and unassailable technical verifications.
- Create an official evidentiary basis that can be used by parliamentarians, researchers, the media
-   and the courts.
- The Secretariat is not a civil or criminal court issuing fines or direct construction injunctions
-   against private companies.
- Its authority is exercised at the higher level of interstate accountability and transparency of
-   environmental public action.

The CCE Secretariat is not a court of first instance, but a higher international institution of
transparency and control of law enforcement. The major strength of the SEM process lies in its
unique capacity to establish an incontestable public documentary truth, instructed by independent
experts, forcing the State to answer for its actions before its trinational partners of the ACEUM
and international public opinion.

## Part 05 The complaint, allegation by allegation

The revised communication is 15 pages long, accompanied by an analytical annex of 102 pages and more
than 200 documents. It was signed in Blainville on July 16, 2026 and ends with this sentence:
“Progress is not measured by the number of profits we generate, but by the number of contaminants we
refuse to leave to our children. »

### Species at risk
The articles, in plain English:
- Art. 32 — prohibits killing, injuring, harassing, capturing or taking an individual of a species
-   listed as extinct, endangered or threatened.
- Art. 33 — prohibits damaging or destroying the residence of these individuals (their nest, their
-   burrow, their hibernacula).
- Art. 58 — prohibits destruction of critical habitat. Crucial point: this ban applies to aquatic
-   species and migratory birds even outside federal lands. It is the gateway to private land.
- Art. 73 — this is the article of the permit. The Minister may authorize an activity affecting a
-   listed species, but only if all other options have been considered, if all minimization measures
-   are taken, and if the survival of the species is not jeopardized. The decision must be reasoned
-   and entered in the public register.
- Art. 79 — requires anyone assessing the environmental effects of a project to notify the Minister if
-   a listed species is likely to be affected.

The reproach: deforestation and backfilling work takes place where species protected by federal law
live, without any permit, any verification of applicability, or any reasoned decision appearing in
public records.

Species documented in the area:
  ▸ Species
  ▸ Status
  ▸ Source
  · Canada Warbler, Bobolink, Wood Thrush, Bank Swallow, Barn Swallow, Eastern Meadowlark
  · Threatened (SARA Schedule 1)
  · Complaint, table 4.1
  · Horned Grebe (Quebec population)
  · Worrying
  · Milk snake
  · Englobe 2020 impact study
  · Little Brown Bat, Northern Myotis, Eastern Pipistrelle

  · Whip-poor-will

  · Impact study: “extensively uses the area for feeding and potentially for nesting”; males heard singing on territory
  · Common Nightjar, Eastern Wood-Pewee

  · Four-toed salamander — 27 specimens observed in May 2016
  · Not at risk (federal) / Likely to be designated threatened or vulnerable (Quebec)
  · Marsh inventories MH-5, MH-19, MH-20, MH-24
  · Black ash
  · Recommended by COSEWIC

  · Painted turtle, “found in abundance”
  · With federal status
  · Pileated Woodpecker, Great Blue Heron, Green Heron
  · Observed during inventories
  · Request for commitments from MELCCFP 2024

Clarification on the Four-toed Salamander: The file rigorously distinguishes between provincial and
federal statuses. The 27 specimens recorded in the marshes MH-5, MH-19, MH-20 and MH-24
(standardized protocol Ouellet, 2005), designated likely to be threatened or vulnerable in Quebec,
are included as biological indicators attesting to the integrity and high ecological sensitivity of
the sphagnum mats on the site.

The three basic arguments:
- The deforestation schedule is biologically absurd. The authorization requires the deforestation to
-   be completed before April 15, in order to avoid the bird nesting season. But, underlines an expert
-   from the Ecomuseum Zoo cited in the file, the cold-blooded species are then still immobile: the
-   turtles hibernate, the snakes are in their hibernacula, the salamanders are under the sphagnum
-   moss. They are inCCEssible, therefore invisible, therefore impossible to save. Protect birds by
-   crushing reptiles.
- The inventory was taken in the wrong season. The natural environment characterization study,
-   validated by provincial authorities in October 2023, was carried out in the middle of autumn.
-   During this period, neither the nesting of birds nor the reproduction of amphibians and reptiles
-   can be observed. The portrait is therefore structurally incomplete — and it is this portrait which
-   served as the basis for the authorization.
- There is no trace of a permit. The absence is deduced from consultation of the Public Registry of
-   Species at Risk, public statements from DFO to the media, and a request for access to information
-   addressed to the Ville de Blainville, whose official response of June 30, 2026 indicates that it
-   does not hold any documentary trace of a federal permit, wildlife studies or environmental
-   monitoring emanating from Ottawa for this site.

### Migratory birds
- Art. 5.1(1) of the Act — prohibits the discharge of a substance harmful to migratory birds into the
-   waters or regions they frequent, or into any other place from which the substance could reach
-   them. This last clarification is decisive: it is not necessary to discharge directly into the
-   pond; the product just needs to be able to get there.
- Art. 5(1) of the Regulations — prohibits, unless permitted: (a) capturing, killing, injuring or
-   harassing a migratory bird; b) destroy or disturb an egg; c) damage, destroy, remove or disturb a
-   nest.

The heaviest word in this regulation is “disturb”. It is not necessary to kill a bird or destroy a
nest: disturbance is enough. It is on this word that the argument for work carried out during the
nesting period is based.

What is being said:
- 132 species of birds recorded during the breeding season in the plots of the Atlas of Breeding Birds
-   of Quebec corresponding to the sector; according to the 2025 provincial report, 66% were breeding
-   and 34% probable or possible breeding.
- During specific surveys in June 2016: 172 individuals of 51 species of protected migratory birds
-   counted in the planned cutting area — including the Red-winged Blackbird, the Common Yellowthroat,
-   the Fox Thrush, the Oven-crowned Warbler, the White-throated Sparrow.
- On May 22, 2026, during the critical period of protected nesting, citizens photographed the arrival
-   of a semi-trailer delivering several industrial lighting towers to the construction site. With
-   strict factual rigor, the file distinguishes the material proof of the deployment of nighttime
-   equipment from their continued use, while demonstrating the direct risk of light disturbance
-   within the meaning of Article 5(1) of the Regulation.
- Mortality of common loons, protected migratory birds, reported by Radio-Canada Enquête on October 9,
-   2025.
- Documented releases of cadmium, chromium and suspended solids into a network connected to the stream
-   — precisely the scenario targeted by section 5.1(1).
- No ECCC permits identified in public records.

An element that the Secretariat itself noted: a document from the Quebec Ministry of the
Environment, the 2024 “request for commitments”, requires Stablex to take nesting periods into
account, explicitly refers to ECCC tools to avoid harming migratory birds, and asks it to “present
measures to be put in place to protect the nests” of the pileated woodpecker, the great blue heron
and the green heron before the end of the period waiting period provided for in federal regulations.
In other words: Quebec referred to the federal framework. Which makes the absence of any trace of
federal intervention all the more striking.

### The Fisheries Act
This is the heart of the case, and by far the most powerful legal weapon. For what ? Because these
prohibitions apply regardless of who owns the land. The Loi 93 was able to transfer ownership, it
did not change the fact that there are fish downstream.
- Art. 34.4 — prohibits the exploitation of a work, business or activity that results in the death of
-   fish (except fishing itself), unless authorized.
- Art. 35 — prohibits any activity resulting in the deterioration, destruction or disturbance of fish
-   habitat, unless authorized by the Minister. It is the most cited article in Canada on wetlands.
- Art. 36(3) — prohibits the dumping or discharge of a harmful substance into waters frequented by
-   fish, or in any other place from which it may reach them. Here again: the gap matters.
- Art. 36(4) — provides for exceptions: discharges authorized by regulation, in specific quantities
-   and conditions. Added to the analysis in the second decision.
- Art. 37 — allows the Minister to require documents from the operator, then modify, restrict or cease
-   the activity.
- Art. 38 — organizes inspections. It also creates an obligation to report: everyone must report to
-   the authorities the death of fish, the destruction of their habitat or the unauthorized discharge
-   of a harmful substance, take corrective measures and produce a report.

#### a) Are there really fish?
This is the first thing a government could challenge. The response to the file is massive.
Scientific fishing carried out in August 2016 in the site's ditches — using electricity and fishing
rods — captured 155 specimens of 9 species: mudfish (108), redfin shiner (17), fivespine stickleback
(14), pumpkinseed, white sucker, pearl mullet, yellow-bellied darter, fathead minnow.

Better: it is the study of the promoter himself which recognizes that “the fish habitat is
considered potential over the entire length of the watercourse studied”. She specifies that “all of
the streams in the study area are in fact ditches […] and the water in the ditches comes from
wetlands present on the outskirts. It flows slowly towards natural streams located outside the study
area.” Some of these ditches include mature aquatic grass beds and beaver reservoirs reaching 2
meters deep. A 2020 government inspection report confirms a network of interconnected ditches
actively draining eastward.

Logical conclusion of the file: it is not an isolated hole in the middle of a field. It is a
connected hydraulic system, which leads to Lockhead Creek, then to Fauvel Lakes. Anything that falls
in travels.

#### b) Contamination measures
  ▸ What
  ▸ When
  ▸ How much
  ▸ Who measured
  · Cadmium in surface water
  · Dec 2 2023
  · 0.016 mg/L = 320 times the protection threshold for aquatic life
  · Citizen sampling
  · Cadmium in water, station FS-STB-2
  · Oct.-Nov. 2025
  · 0.0014 mg/L = 4.7 times the threshold
  · WaterShed Monitoring, ICP-MS analyzes
  · Cadmium in sediments
  · 31.8 times (up to 57.6 according to the detailed table) the rare endpoint
  · WaterShed Monitoring
  · Chromium in sediment
  · 14.23 times the rare endpoint
  · Turbidity/suspended solids
  · 4.2 to 8.1 times the threshold
  · Metals after road spill
  · August 21, 2025
  · 17 metals out of 17 sought; lead ×42, mercury ×38, cadmium ×4 CMM standards
  · Bureau Veritas
  · Arsenic, copper, zinc
  · dec. 2023
  · Exceedings of standards
  · Independent surface campaign

What do these thresholds mean? The CVAC (chronic aquatic life criterion) is the concentration beyond
which prolonged exposure harms aquatic organisms. The CER (rare effect criterion) applies to
sediments: it is the level below which practically no toxic effects are expected. Exceeding a
threshold does not automatically mean that there is a violation — it means that there is a serious
reason to check.

#### c) Incidents, in order
- February 11, 2013 — fire involving hazardous materials at 760, boulevard Industriel: combustion of
-   wood, plastics, glass and used activated carbon contaminated with chrome, lead and cadmium
-   (Urgence-Environnement file T-15-2013-02-11-292).
- June 11, 2021 — major industrial fire at the same location. Mobilization of three fire departments
-   and the Laurentides Public Health Department. Release estimated at 20 tonnes of nitric acid and
-   chlorinated solids affecting the air and ground (file 301537240).
- August 21, 2025 — spill estimated at more than 1,300 liters; 17 metals detected.
- March 13 to April 15, 2026 — fish mortality at Lacs Fauvel: cyprinids, centrarchids and northern
-   pike, photographed and time-stamped, with ocher and rust coloring characteristic of water and ice.
- April 14, 2026 — hydraulic oil spill at 755 Industriel Boulevard. Locke Head Creek is directly
-   affected; intervention by Urgence-Environnement, the City and the fire department (file
-   301886321).
- June 18, 2026 — overflow of containment ditches towards the creek after ordinary summer
-   precipitation (file 301899969, release of suspended solids).
- July 2, 2026 — recurrence noted by citizens after 20 to 30 mm of rain, observations around 10 p.m.,
-   “identical in every way” to the event of June 18.

The argument drawn from these last two events is frighteningly simple: if the containment measures
fail in an ordinary summer rain, they are not designed for the actual climate of the site.

#### d) Three angles which make the strength of the file
1. “Anoxia is an effect, not a cause. »

The Ville de Blainville explained the fish mortality by a lack of oxygen (press release of April 15,
2026). The expert cited in the file turns the argument around: “Anoxia, lack of oxygen, is an
EFFECT. To kill someone from anoxia, I can strangle you, or submerge your head in a bucket of water.
Death will be anoxia. But what CAUSED the anoxia? »

And the file documents a crumbling of the municipal version: first presented as a “scientific
certainty” confirmed by a private firm, the hypothesis becomes a simple “hypothesis” at the council
meeting of July 7, before the mayor admits that the City did not have any results of its own
analysis and that competence fell to the ministry. The request for access to information of June 30,
2026 confirms this: the legal affairs department responds that “no document corresponding to what
you are looking for” has been found. The file also notes that the mayor attributed the rusty color
of the water to “probably ferrous”, without scientific qualification, and that she refused to issue
a preventive advisory against swimming for the summer of 2026.

2. The fox who guards the henhouse.

The file supports, on the basis of corroborating testimonies and public documents: the private
Stablex laboratory is accredited by the Quebec ministry, which gives it the status of an independent
laboratory; sampling at observation wells is carried out jointly by the Ministry and Stablex, the
Ministry not having independent equipment; the analyzes are carried out in the Stablex laboratory.
In other words, the regulated entity analyzes its own pollution. The file also mentions a practice
of pumping wells before re-sampling, which would have the effect of diluting the concentrations, and
divergent results for the same well depending on whether they come from the Ministry or the company.
The author draws a legal conclusion: in the absence of any independent federal inspection, Ottawa
bases its abstention on data produced by the operator itself.

He also points out a discharge pipe located at the rear of the installation, discharging effluent
into a road ditch connected to Lockheed Creek and Lake Fauvel, which would not appear in the
official sampling protocol.

3. The missing declaration.

Stablex's 2024 report to the National Pollutant Release Inventory — a mandatory federal registry —
does not report any releases to any watercourse. The company publicly claims “zero discharge into
water for 26 years”. However, the file contrasts this with the overflows of June and July 2026, the
analyzes of October-November 2025 and the spill of August 2025. A cited expert makes the tax
analogy: “if you do not declare a rejection, it can be considered fraud, in the same way as when you
declare your income to Revenue Canada”. The question asked is simple: did ECCC even verify?

### Toxic substances and the right to a healthy environment
A major legal novelty, still little known. Since the Right to a Healthy Environment Act, assented to
on June 13, 2023, CEPA requires the federal government to “protect the right of every individual in
Canada to a healthy environment […] subject to reasonable limits”, respecting the principles of
non-regression, intergenerational equity and environmental justice — which includes the prevention
of harmful effects disproportionately affecting vulnerable populations.

Since 2023, there has been a legal right to a healthy environment in Canada. And this right must be
applied with future generations in mind. An adolescent who invokes this right for himself and for
those who come after him is therefore not using a figure of speech: he is relying on a brand new
legal text, which no one has yet really tested. This is, legally, the most innovative aspect of the
case.
- Between 2019 and 2024, the site would have received 120,837 tonnes of soil from the Orrington site
-   (Maine, United States), linked to the Mallinckrodt company. 84% of this volume — 101,951 tonnes —
-   would be classified as hazardous residue due to its mercury content. Mercury is a very volatile
-   metal.
- These operations take place 370 to 400 meters from residential and commercial areas.
- The company's release inventory lists fine particles PM2.5, hexavalent chromium (recognized
-   carcinogen), mercury, arsenic, nickel, crystalline silica and ammonia. An Englobe report would
-   confirm the physical presence of hexavalent chromium and crystalline silica in the dust from the
-   site.
- The framework for implementing the right to a healthy environment, required by section 5.1(1) of
-   CEPA, was due no later than June 13, 2025.

The criticism: ECCC would not have exercised any of its powers of evaluation (art. 68), collection
of information (art. 71) or risk management (art. 90 and 91) with regard to this site.

### 5.5 — Cross-cutting arguments
Articulation with commercial (art. 24.4) and constitutional (art. 35) law. The communication
highlights the overall context of the matter: on the one hand, the competitive distortion created by
the massive importation of American waste without rigorous application of federal standards; on the
other hand, the location of the site on the traditional unceded territory of the Kanien'kehá:ka
Nation and the principles of the two-row Wampum. If the Secretariat has limited the formal scope of
the investigation to only federal “environmental laws” in the strict sense of Article 24.1 (thus
excluding the aspects relating to Chapter 31 on commerce or general constitutional law), this
contextual insight remains in the public register to document the extent of the issues raised.

The Loi 93 as a lock. This is the most political argument. When a provincial government uses its
parliament to neutralize local counter-powers and close legal recourse, the author maintains, the
application of federal laws becomes “the one and only bulwark”. Ottawa's inaction would then create
“a total legal vacuum”. It relies on precedents from CCE (British Columbia Mines, SEM-98-004;
British Columbia Salmon Farms, SEM-12-001) where the existence of provincial authorizations was not
considered sufficient to exempt the federal government from acting.

The “discretion/absence of decision” distinction. This is the finest reasoning in the file. Article
24.4(2) of ACEUM explicitly recognizes that a country retains the right to exercise discretion in
matters of investigation and allocation of resources; acting reasonably in exercising this
discretion is not a violation. The author concedes this from the outset - then turns the argument
around: we can only speak of a discretionary choice if a power has first been exercised. Here, there
would be no inspection, no refusal, no reasoned decision. So not misused discretion, but a pure and
simple absence of exercise. And he adds: Canada's response will decide itself — documented
inspections would enlighten the Secretariat, while its silence would reinforce the thesis of
sustained inaction.

The argument a fortiori on the precedents of the CCE. In the historic case SEM-97-005, Canada was
the subject of a factual case for failure to apply the Fisheries Act (discharge of acids into the
St. Lawrence). Environment Canada then formally documented its refusal to intervene. William Guindon
pertinently demonstrates that the situation in Blainville reveals an even more obvious deficiency:
here, the federal apparatus has not even rendered a reasoned decision or recorded a formal
evaluation, characterizing a total and persistent omission to apply the law.

### 5.6 — The personal injury invoked
Three components: environmental (destruction of wetlands which constitute its immediate
environment); health (exposure to contaminants in surface water and sediments near one's place of
residence); intergenerational (the perpetual burial of dangerous materials that he and future
generations will inherit). The summary table in the complaint places it 3.5 km from the site.

### 5.7 — What he asks, concretely
He is not asking for condemnation. It asks the Secretariat to declare the complaint admissible, to
request a detailed response from Canada for each of the four laws, to verify the existence of
permits, and to assess whether there is a need to compile a factual file. It specifically requests
clarification on: the compatibility of the deforestation schedule with the biology of cold-blooded
species; measures taken after fish mortality; the exercise of the powers of sections 37 and 38 of
the Fisheries Act. And he expressly specifies that he is not asking the Secretariat to rule on the
civil, criminal or administrative liability of Stablex or Quebec.

### 5.8 — Exemplary ethical rigor
In section 15 of his communication, William Guindon demonstrates remarkable intellectual probity. It
precisely details the contribution of the experts and specialists consulted — professor-researcher
in environmental hydrology, biodiversity analyst, citizen representatives and representatives of the
Kanien'kehá:ka Nation — while assuming sole and full responsibility for its analyzes and legal
conclusions. This scrupulous transparency reinforces the credibility and independence of its civic
approach.

### 5.9 — Field elements documented in the file
The elements below summarize the solid body of documentation assembled by William Guindon:
ministerial inspection reports, university hydrogeological studies, sampling campaigns in accredited
laboratories and georeferenced photographic surveys.

#### a) Provincial inspection report No. 402462402 (April 2025)
- 10 m riparian protection zone planned: marking completely absent on the ground at the time of the
-   observation.
- Traffic of heavy machinery: observed approximately 5 m from the unnamed watercourse, in
-   contravention of the prescribed setback strips.
- Management of common reed (Phragmites australis): unmarked colonized sectors and uncleaned machinery
-   leaving the contaminated area, risking accelerating biological invasion.
- Five distinct breaches of the decree identified: provincial intervention closed on the
-   recommendation of a simple notice of non-compliance, on the administrative grounds of the “short
-   length of bank affected” and the nature of an “already disturbed site”.
