# Consultative Framework of Experts and Scientific Independence
Methodology for verifying scientific data and independence of expertise

## 1. Purpose and transversal scope

This methodological framework governs the way in which William Guindon and his site base their
analyzes and assertions of a scientific and technical nature within the framework of the SEM-26-003
submission, international representations (CCE, UN), open letters, public interventions and
documentary publications.

William Guindon, as a citizen, student and submitter of the international citizen communication
SEM-26-003 to the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United
States–Mexico Agreement)), does not present himself as a scientist. The issues addressed (protection
of Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog), management of hazardous materials,
hydrogeology, ecotoxicology of cadmium) require absolute methodological rigor and the support of
verifiable primary sources.

## 2. Principles of scientific independence

- Scientific data and conclusions are reported faithfully, without distortion, and referenced
-   (studies, public reports, standards, methodological measurements).
- The opinion of an expert is distinguished from the facts and presented as such.
- The site does not solicit paid advice conditional on a pre-established conclusion. The experts
-   consulted are for their competence, with disclosure of links of interest where applicable.
- William does not receive scientific instruction from any party (neither business, nor government,
-   nor interest group).
- In the event of scientific uncertainty, this is recognized; the site avoids false balance and
-   misleading presentation.

## 3. Types of expertise consulted

The site can consult, according to needs:
- scientists and researchers (biology, ecology, hydrology, toxicology, public health, environmental
-   law);
- public and independent organizations, recognized associations;
- legal experts (lawyers from the Quebec Bar);
- statisticians and specialists capable of validating the measurements and their reading.

## 4. Technical proofreading and legal advice

When an article relies heavily on scientific data or on an allegation of non-compliance with a
standard, William strives to have the article (or its technical aspects) proofread by a competent
expert, before publication, in order to reduce the risk of error.

A rereading by an expert does not transform William into an expert nor the article into a scientific
publication, but strengthens its reliability.

## 5. Editorial independence

Consultation of experts does not entail any transfer of editorial control: the choices of angle,
wording and publication remain those of William, under the supervision of his parents.

This framework is a standalone policy of williamguindon.me. No signature is required.

Title : Halloween — Real monsters don't wear masks | William Guindon

# Real monsters don't wear masks.
This week everyone is looking for ghosts, monsters and witches. In Blainville, the facts lying
dormant in the ground and brewing in the offices are already sending shivers down the spine.

All week long, we open the classified files. Every night at midnight, a room is decontaminated.

## Six days. Five files. The facts.

A special Halloween series on the Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog):
industrial past, environmental measures, ecological calendar and access to decision-makers. Rooms
are unlocked every night at 00:00.

### Real monsters don't wear masks
This week, everyone is looking for stories about ghosts, monsters and witches to scare themselves.

However, in Blainville, there is no need to invent urban legends: what lies dormant on our floors
and what is brewing in ministerial offices already sends shivers down your spine.

All week long, we open the classified files. Ready for the raw facts?

Title : Ethics and Supervision of AI — William Guindon

# Ethics and Supervision Policy for Artificial Intelligence
Rules for using AI tools, transparency towards readers and editorial responsibility

### Principle position & transversal scope of application
This policy is not limited to the management of the website: it governs all activities, research
procedures, drafting of appeals or submissions (including SEM-26-003), citizen correspondence, open
letters and speaking engagements by William Guindon. “No content intended for the public or an
official authority is produced without full human control, and any algorithmic assistance remains
under the exclusive human supervision of William and his parents. » AI is a work support tool, never
a source of truth nor an autonomous author. Responsibility for any approach or publication remains
entirely human.

## 1. Purpose and scope

This policy governs the responsible and ethical use of computer technologies and artificial
intelligence in the context of:
- documentary research and the organization of evidentiary data;
- the drafting of citizen submissions, briefs, press releases and open letters;
- electronic communications and public correspondence;
- tools and services deployed on the williamguindon.me site.

## 2. Terms and framework for using AI infrastructures

I provide strict and functional supervision of IT technologies. Artificial intelligence tools are
used exclusively as technical support instruments, documentary research and software assistance, in
order to compensate for the substantial asymmetry of resources in the analysis of environmental
files.

### 2.1 Documentary analysis and exploration of complex corpora
I use indexing, semantic search and data processing technologies (notably full-text search engines
and cutting-edge language models) to identify, categorize and cross-reference evidentiary passages
within voluminous documentary sets (hydrogeological studies, authorization orders, technical
reports). These tools only intervene in the localization of textual elements; the legal
qualification, the interpretation of the facts and the writing of theses remain under my exclusive
human appreciation.

### 2.2 Running local models for common tasks
For elementary linguistic operations that do not require any remote computing power — such as
syntactic revision, optimization of sentence structures and occasional textual corrections — I give
priority to the Bonsai 27B model (derived from the Qwen open source architecture), executed entirely
locally on my computer hardware. This protocol ensures the strict confidentiality of the data
processed and prevents any unnecessary transfer of requests to third-party servers.

### 2.3 Technical assistance for source code and web tools
I use models to assist in the development, debugging and standardization of programming files (HTML,
style sheets, functional scripts) hosted on my domain. Each generated software or algorithmic
component is subject to a functional audit and manual validation prior to its integration into
production.

### 2.4 Proportionality and environmental assessment
The energy consumption induced by the hosted calculations (estimated at a few tens of kilowatt hours
on the Hydro-Québec network, which is largely carbon-free) meets a criterion of material
proportionality. This recourse is limited to steps aimed at documenting and preventing permanent and
large-scale environmental damage (in particular the protection of wetlands and the control of
dangerous materials).

## 3. Prohibited uses

- Publish a text, image, sound or video substantially generated by generative AI without identifying
-   them as such (principle 14.7 of the Quebec Press Council).
- Present unverified AI output as verified fact (models can generate factual errors).
- Using AI to fabricate evidence, alter documents or create false testimonies.
- Transmit personal, confidential or sensitive information (sources, internal documents, data of
-   minors) to an AI model.
- Relying on AI for editorial decisions or to distort source statements.

## 4. Human editorial control — procedure

Before any publication or sending:
- The final content is written or edited by William from verified sources;
- Any AI output used is reread, cross-referenced with the sources and corrected by William;
- Facts, figures, quotes and documents are checked against the original sources, never against AI
-   output alone;
- Social media posts and emails prepared with AI assistance are always double-checked by William
-   before sending/publishing;
- Any published content substantially generated by AI is visibly and inseparably identified.

## 5. Transparency, attribution and primacy of human inquiry

Automated systems are by nature incapable of carrying out a legal investigation process, determining
an evidentiary strategy or understanding the material reality of the premises. The entire
investigation of my files is based on my personal investigation work:
- Archive examination and evidentiary conservation: I myself carry out the identification in the
-   public archives of historical documents, judgments or court reports not indexed by the usual
-   search engines, of which I ensure the manual capture, temporal authentication, classification and
-   inclusion in the proof annexes.
- Exercise of rights of access to information: I personally ensure the formulation, legal
-   qualification, formal filing and monitoring of requests for access to documents from public bodies
-   as well as related administrative appeals with provincial and federal authorities.
- Direct obtaining of technical documents: I obtained and assembled administrative correspondence,
-   specialized hydrogeological studies and engineering plans not publicly disseminated by direct
-   approaches to information custodians.
- Physical findings in the field: I conducted the in situ examination of topography, receiving
-   watercourses and threatened wetlands, while personally cross-checking these observations with
-   geospatial data and consulting with independent scientific experts.
- Counter-expertise and detection of anomalies: I myself identify methodological deviations,
-   toxicological anomalies and excesses of standards in industrial and government reports.
- Legal theory and formulation of appeals: I developed all of the legal argumentation in support of my
-   efforts (in particular under the mechanisms of ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico
-   Agreement)/CUSMA). AI does not intervene at any time in the design of the theses defended.
- Methodological transparency: My use of data processing technologies is fully explained. It
-   constitutes a method of technical organization proportionate to ensuring the exhaustive processing
-   of thousands of regulatory pages.
- Traceability and opposability by primary sources: The validity of my work is based on the probative
-   force of officially referenced official acts, engineering studies and legal provisions. No
-   assertion arises from autonomous algorithmic processing not corroborated by a verifiable material
-   source.
- Accountability and personal editorial responsibility: Any content made public or filed before a
-   court or an international organization engages my exclusive personal responsibility, without
-   intellectual delegation or release of liability.

## 6. Liability

The site remains solely editorially responsible for all published content, including if an error
comes from an AI tool. The use of an AI tool is not an excuse and does not waive the rules of
truthfulness and accuracy.

## 7. AI Documentary Assistant — Formal exclusion of liability, independence and absence of legal value

### Formal Warning & Strict Legal Disclaimer
The AI ​​Document Assistant available on this site is an automated third-party computer tool,
strictly separate from William Guindon. This tool is made available to the public to facilitate
documentary research and consultation of public documents in the SEM-26-003 file. Its use is subject
to the strict and enforceable conditions set out below.

Any person accessing or conversing with the AI ​​Documentary Assistant expressly acknowledges and
agrees to the following provisions:
- Absolute statutory distinction: The AI ​​Documentary Assistant is an automated algorithmic system
-   and has no status as agent, spokesperson or representative of William Guindon, his relatives or
-   his technical advisors. Statements or responses produced by the assistant in no way reflect the
-   opinions, wishes or statements of William Guindon.
- Total absence of legal advice or evidentiary authority: This automated assistant does not replace
-   professional legal advice, legal advice from an attorney or official scientific analysis. The
-   summaries generated have no legal or probative value before the Commission de coopération
-   environnementale (CCE / ACEUM), the courts, the BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental
-   Hearings) or any government ministry or agency. Only briefs, decisions and supporting documents
-   officially listed and filed in the register are authentic.
- Strict confidentiality & Prohibition of posting sensitive data: Do not submit any confidential
-   information, personally identifiable information, identifying information, sensitive documents or
-   trade secrets in this chat. Conversations are processed by software interfaces (local browser or
-   language model API). William Guindon cannot be held responsible for the information that users
-   voluntarily choose to enter in the chat interface.
- Full exclusion of liability: William Guindon, his relatives and his collaborators disclaim any
-   direct, indirect or subsidiary liability for any factual errors, omissions, approximations,
-   algorithmic hallucinations or erroneous conclusions generated by the AI ​​Documentary Assistant,
-   as well as for any decision or action taken on the basis of its responses.

## 8. Absolute confidentiality and non-consultation of personal API keys (BYO-API)

### Formal commitment: Your API keys remain 100% local and private
William Guindon has no access to, does not store on any server, and never sees your API keys or
personal access tokens (Google Gemini, OpenAI, Anthropic Claude, Mistral AI, OpenRouter, Hugging
Face, etc.).
- Storage exclusively confined to the user's terminal: Your API keys and local instance addresses
-   (Ollama, LM Studio) are saved exclusively within your own browser's local sandbox (multi-level
-   localStorage, IndexedDB and CacheStorage to resist session cookie dumps without ever leaving your
-   machine).
- Direct and encrypted client-provider connection: The requests made pass directly and without
-   intermediary from your browser to the official gateways of AI providers (e.g.
-   generativelanguage.googleapis.com, api.openai.com, api.anthropic.com, api.mistral.ai,
-   openrouter.ai). No proxy, mirror server or log managed by William Guindon intercepts, sees or logs
-   your identifiers or the content of your exchanges.
- Control and right to immediate oblivion: You can view your key in plain text at any time using the
-   display button (), modify it or irrevocably purge it from your browser via the “Forget” button.

This policy is a stand-alone policy of williamguindon.me, published for public transparency. No
signature is required.

# Declaration of Political Independence and Financial Integrity
Political independence, financial integrity and transversal editorial commitments of William Guindon

## PART A — POLITICAL INDEPENDENCE, CITIZEN AUTONOMY AND EXTERNAL RELATIONS

### A.1 Purpose and fundamental mission
The williamguindon.me site and all the initiatives led by William Guindon have the exclusive mission
of information of public interest, factual environmental monitoring and the creation of an
independent citizen documentary register. William Guindon, author and exclusive submitter of the
international citizen communication SEM-26-003 to the Secretariat of the Commission de coopération
environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement)), acts as a strictly
autonomous citizen investigator. He is not the agent, spokesperson, candidate, member of an
executive committee, nor affiliate of any political party, level of government, public organization
or private corporate entity.

### A.2 Strict autonomy from political parties
- Absence of partisan affiliation: William Guindon does not support, promote or adhere to any
-   political party (federal, provincial or municipal). No affinity or occasional convergence can
-   constitute allegiance or partisan commitment.
- Strictly professional institutional interactions: Any exchange with elected representatives,
-   political attachés or civil servants takes place exclusively within the formal framework of
-   sharing information of public interest, transparent, limited and strictly professional.
- Refusal of any interference: No editorial instructions, guidance or suggestions may be sought or
-   received from any political party or authority.
- Prohibition of propaganda: The site and associated channels formally refuse any paid advertising,
-   electoral sponsorship or partisan content.

### A.3 Clear distinction from local activist coalitions and committees
- Absence of collective affiliation: Although citizen coalitions, vigilance committees or activist
-   groups can intervene on related environmental issues, William Guindon maintains complete
-   operational and organizational disassociation. He does not participate in any management
-   committee, receives no instructions and in no way acts as a spokesperson for a collective.
- Probative independence: The published analyses, legal theses and access to information procedures
-   are conducted in an isolated and objective manner, without prior strategic consultation with
-   pressure groups.

### A.4 Reuse framework and prohibition of instrumentalization
- Fair Sharing Encouraged: Fair quoting, sharing and republishing of public records is permitted
-   subject to clear and complete attribution of the source (William Guindon / williamguindon.me).
- Prohibition of alteration and truncation: It is strictly prohibited to extract segments out of
-   context, to alter, to truncate or to manipulate the writings of William Guindon, in particular the
-   official submission SEM-26-003 and its evidentiary annexes.
- Prohibition of promotional or partisan use: The exploitation of William Guindon's work for the
-   purposes of third-party fundraising, electoral campaigning, political tracts or corporate
-   self-promotion is strictly prohibited.
- Documents in the public domain and official response from Canada: Concerning government acts and the
-   formal response expected from Canada (in particular for the deadline of October 16, 2026 under
-   SEM-26-003), although these are public documents, any distorted interpretation, partisan recovery
-   or misleading comment by third parties will be the subject of an immediate and documented public
-   clarification by William Guindon.

### A.5 Official notice of public clarification — Lack of collaboration and strict independence (September 24, 2026)
Following recurring questions and in order to dispel any ambiguity as to the origin and execution of
the work comprising the international file SEM-26-003 filed with the Secretariat of the Commission
de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM), the author formally records the following factual
details:
- Absence of assistance, co-editorship or political affiliation: William Guindon received no
-   assistance, guidance or collaboration from the Climat Québec political party, its leader, or the
-   Coalition of Blainville Citizens against Cell #6 of Stablex. These entities did not draft, fund or
-   supervise any section of the submission. Any allegation or public statement asserting or implying
-   that assistance was provided in the preparation or documentation of the file is formally
-   inaccurate.
- Absence of support from sponsored external researchers: Contrary to certain statements made, no
-   external university researcher or specialist in analytical chemistry associated with the sampling
-   campaigns contributed, guided or participated in the citizen approach led by William Guindon.
- Clarification relating to the elements transmitted: The only documentary contact that took place
-   with a member of the citizen coalition was limited to the receipt of around twenty archive
-   photographs. These images — moreover already publicly available on social networks — were not
-   retained or included in the official file filed with the CCE (Commission for Environmental
-   Cooperation), due to lack of scientific data or probative value required for the analysis.
- Individual and self-financed nature: SEM-26-003 communication is the exclusive fruit of personal,
-   individual and independent work. In accordance with the provisions of this declaration (in
-   particular parts A.2, A.3 and A.4), any use of the name, research or image of William Guindon for
-   partisan, promotional or electoral purposes remains strictly unauthorized.

The analyzes and procedures submitted to national and international authorities are rigorously based
on verifiable data, free from any electoral or partisan dynamics.

## PART B — FINANCIAL INTEGRITY, TECHNICAL RESOURCES AND ABSENCE OF CONFLICTS OF INTEREST

### B.1 Self-financing of research, professional fees and absence of donations
- Self-financing of research and register: The activities of documentary research, environmental
-   analysis, web hosting, postal franking and access to administrative documents are self-financed by
-   William Guindon from his personal funds (work savings and personal family amounts). The SEM-26-003
-   registry and the website do not solicit or receive monetary donations, public grants or private
-   sponsorships.
- Professional fees for conferences and public interventions: William Guindon receives legitimate
-   professional fees and reimbursement of his travel expenses as part of his conferences, expert
-   panels, school talks or popular education speaking engagements. These services do not grant any
-   right of review, interference, editorial direction or veto to anyone on its citizen surveys or on
-   communication CCE SEM-26-003.
- Exploitation of free and academic resources: In order to compensate for the asymmetry of resources
-   faced by large organizations without financial dependence, the infrastructure is based exclusively
-   on free access tools, open research quotas, the GitHub Student Developer Pack student program,
-   open source software architectures and volunteer mentoring without financial or editorial
-   compensation.
- Absolute refusal of any third-party intermediary or material collection: No third-party donation,
-   prize pool or material list service is active on the site. William Guindon himself ensures the
-   acquisition of his naturalist inventory and citizen analysis equipment in order to guarantee total
-   integrity, verifiable and free from any external influence.

### B.2 Strict supervision of possible future micro-contributions
In the event that a citizen micro-contribution mechanism would be deployed to absorb increasing
administrative costs (via a legal platform adapted to minors supervised by their parents, such as
Mydoh / Royal Bank of Canada):
- Categorical exclusion of legal entities: Absolute prohibition on receiving contributions from
-   private companies, industrial lobbies, trade union organizations or political parties.
- Refusal by covered entities: Any donation emanating directly or indirectly from a natural or legal
-   person linked to the investigation files processed (in particular the hazardous waste management
-   sector or the targeted promoters) will be immediately rejected or refunded.
- Ceiling and transparency: Contributions will be subject to a strict ceiling per individual, with
-   refusal of excessive anonymous contributions. A transparent register of amounts collected will be
-   kept up to date.
- Absence of compensation: Any monetary support confers strictly no right of review, orientation or
-   veto over the editorial line or investigative choices.

### B.3 Family integrity and absolute absence of conflicts of interest
- Declaration of honor: The author certifies that no member of his immediate family circle holds a
-   position, holds financial interests, sits on a board of directors or is employed by a company, a
-   ministry or an organization directly implicated in his submissions or investigation files (in
-   particular Stablex Canada inc., the MEIE, the MELCCFP (Québec Ministry of the Environment, the
-   Fight against Climate Change, Wildlife and Parks) or ECCC).
- Commitment to lasting neutrality: No past, present or foreseeable professional or financial
-   connection from those around them compromises, influences or directs the objectivity of the
-   factual conclusions published.

## PART C — EDITORIAL COMMITMENTS, VERIFICATION AND DEFENSE OF FAIR COMMENT

### C.1 Good faith, public interest and legal protection
- Standard of good faith: All assertions, publications and actions of William Guindon are guided by
-   good faith, the search for material truth and the protection of the public interest (collective
-   health, preservation of wetlands and respect for environmental laws).
- Absence of personal animosity: The procedures aim exclusively at the analysis of administrative
-   decisions, the assessment of environmental impacts and the legal compliance of industrial
-   installations, without animosity, intention to harm, harassment or defamation.
- Protection against SLAPP lawsuits (Anti-SLAPP): The work falls fully within the protection given to
-   debates of public interest, in accordance with the principles of freedom of expression and the
-   provisions of articles 51 et seq. of the Code of Civil Procedure of Quebec repressing abusive
-   prosecutions or attempts at judicial intimidation.

### C.2 Responsibility for publication and rectification protocol
- Exclusive responsibility: William Guindon assumes full editorial responsibility for his own texts,
-   analyzes and publications.
- Right of factual rectification: Any person, organization or representative mentioned believing that
-   a publication contains a demonstrable factual error is invited to make a reasoned request for
-   correction in writing (via the site contact form or by official email).
- Diligent processing: Any error report is carefully examined. When a material inaccuracy is confirmed
-   on the basis of verifiable primary sources, a correction or explanatory erratum note is publicly
-   and promptly incorporated into the article concerned.

### C.3 Exclusion of technical liability
- External links: The site declines all responsibility for the contents, confidentiality policies or
-   technical failures of third-party sites accessible by hypertext links.
- Digital integrity: Although William Guindon ensures the strict safety of hosted documents, it cannot
-   be held responsible for network failures, access interruptions or technical damage arising from
-   the use of global computer networks.

This statement is a stand-alone policy of williamguindon.me, published for editorial transparency.

PAGE 27/50 · https://williamguindon.me/lexique.html
Title : Lexicon & Citizen Dictionary — Understanding the SEM-26-003 file & Stablex | William Guindon
==============================================================================

# Lexicon & Citizen Dictionary
International law, water chemistry and parliamentary procedures often use complicated jargon that
discourages citizens. This page translates into plain language all the acronyms, legal articles,
scientific terms and official documents in the Stablex file and the SEM-26-003 communication, so
that everyone — whatever their age or background — can understand everything.

## ACEUM (CUSMA / USMCA)

It is the major commercial and legal treaty that binds Canada, the United States and Mexico since
2020 (replacing NAFTA). Its Chapter 24 prohibits the three countries from weakening or ignoring
their own environmental laws to favor a company.

## CCE (CEC)

Official international organization created by Canada, the United States and Mexico, headquartered
in Montreal. Its independent Secretariat reviews citizen complaints when one of the three states
fails to effectively enforce its environmental laws.

## SEM-26-003

This is the official file number given by CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) to the
legal complaint filed by William Guindon regarding the expansion of Stablex and the destruction of
Grande Tourbière (Great Peat Bog) de Blainville (“Landfill of hazardous materials in Blainville”).

## Article 24.27 of ACEUM

Rule of the ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement) treaty which allows any citizen to file a
complaint before the CCE (art. 24.27(1)), sets the criteria for validity (art. 24.27(2)) and allows
the Secretariat to oblige the government concerned to respond officially within 60 days (art.
24.27(3) and (4)).

## Article 24.28 of ACEUM

Once the government responds, Article 24.28 gives the CCE Secretariat 60 days to analyze the
response and decide whether to recommend to the Ministers of the Environment of the three countries
(Canada, United States, Mexico) the opening of a thorough investigation called a “Factual File”.

## Factual Record

This is the in-depth investigation report carried out by the scientific and legal experts of the
CCE. It is triggered if at least 2 of the 3 member countries vote for its opening. He publicly
exposes all the facts about non-compliance with environmental laws.

## Exhibit 72-RSP

Official code of the brief filed by the Government of Canada on October 16, 2026 before the CCE. In
this document, Ottawa attempts to close or postpone the SEM-26-003 file by invoking the existence of
a simple citizen petition to the Auditor General.

## Exhibit 73-REC

Official act by which the CCE Secretariat confirms having received Canada's response (72-RSP) on
October 16, 2026 and begins the 60-day countdown provided for in Article 24.28.

## Guideline 7.5 (SEM Procedures)

Official rule of CCE which specifies that a government can only request the cessation of a CCE
investigation if it itself conducts a real trial or a binding inspection capable of resolving the
problem, and not when a citizen has simply asked questions in a petition.

## Loi 93 (Projet de loi 93)

Special law adopted under gag order by the government of Quebec on March 28, 2025. It forces Ville
de Blainville (City of Blainville) to sell its wooded and humid land to the American company Stablex
and dismisses the unfavorable conclusions of BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings).

## Parliamentary gag order

Mechanism by which a majority government cuts short the debates of deputies and public consultations
at the Assemblée nationale (National Assembly of Québec) to have a law adopted in a few hours.

## BAPE (Report 371)

Independent public organization in Quebec responsible for informing and consulting citizens on major
projects. In its Report 371 published in September 2023, the BAPE concluded that the Stablex Cell 6
project in the peatland should not be permitted in its proposed form.

## BVG & CEDD

Independent institution of the federal Parliament in Ottawa which verifies the work of ministries
and manages the register of citizen environmental petitions.

## Environmental Petition No. 546 (Article 22 LVG)

Right provided by federal law allowing a citizen to ask formal questions to federal ministers about
the environment. Ministers have a legal obligation to respond in writing within 120 days, but this
mechanism does not provide the power to order work to stop.

## Cadmium (Cd)

Industrial metal dangerous to fish, wildlife and human health, which does not degrade in nature. In
a sample from the Ministry of the Environment (MELCCFP) taken on December 2, 2023 from the effluent
linked to Stablex activities, cadmium reached 320 times the acute toxicity threshold for aquatic
life.

## Cell 6 (Stablex)

This is the name given to the Stablex expansion project aimed at digging a huge landfill site for
millions of tons of hazardous waste treated until 2065, directly in the heart of a wooded and humid
sector of the Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog).

## Peat bog (and wetland)

A bog is a real natural sponge that has formed over thousands of years. It filters water, prevents
flooding, cools the air and shelters rare plants and animals. Unlike a tree plantation, a peat bog
destroyed by mechanical excavators cannot be recreated artificially.

## Carbon sink

Peatlands store proportionally much more carbon than forests. When a peatland is drained and dug,
the carbon trapped for centuries is released into the atmosphere in the form of greenhouse gases,
worsening climate change.

## Fisheries Act — Section 36(3)

Very powerful Canadian law, section 36(3) of which strictly prohibits the discharge of any toxic or
harmful substance into waters where fish live, or in any place from which this substance risks
flowing into these waters.

## SARA (SARA)

Federal law that protects endangered animal and plant species in Canada and their critical habitat,
with the possibility for the federal government to adopt a safety net or emergency order when the
province does not protect them.

## LCOM (MBCA)

Federal law resulting from an international treaty that prohibits destroying nests, eggs and
migratory birds or degrading protected areas during nesting.

## MELCCFP

This is the Quebec Ministry of the Environment. It was its own laboratory analyzes and internal
technical opinions which documented the severe cadmium exceedances and the very high ecological
value of the peat bog.

## ECCC & MPO

The two federal government departments in Ottawa responsible for enforcing the Fisheries Act
(pollution section), the Species at Risk Act (SARA) and the Migratory Birds Act (MBA).

## Forced expropriation

Normally used to build a school, a hospital or a public road, expropriation was here imposed by the
Loi 93 (Bill 93) to wrest from the Ville de Blainville natural land that it wanted to protect, in
order to transfer it for landfill purposes by a private company.

## Adultism & Civic Capacity

Adultism consists of underestimating or dismissing a person's speech, intelligence or legal actions
simply because they are under 18 years of age. However, the UN Convention on the Rights of the Child
(art. 12) guarantees the right of young people to be heard in decisions that affect their future.

## Leachate & Process water

When rain or groundwater comes into contact with buried or processed materials, it picks up heavy
metals (like cadmium) and other chemicals. This water must be rigorously treated before any
discharge.

# License Policy, Graphic Charter & Photo Inventory
Legal framework for making available under Creative Commons CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 license, official guide

williamguindon.me.

### Fundamental Principle: Default Licensing Rule
Unless expressly stated otherwise (in particular for official public domain documents from CCE
(Commission for Environmental Cooperation), BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings) or
ministries, as well as for third-party software libraries), all original content created by William

International License - No Modification 4.0 International (CC BY-NC-ND 4.0).

This policy governs the rights of reproduction, distribution and quotation for any natural or legal
person, news media, academic researcher or automated system.

-   the source: “William Guindon (williamguindon.me — File SEM-26-003)” with a direct link to the

-   agreement.

-   retouch or distort texts, evidentiary photographs or investigative documents in such a way as to
-   alter their factual or scientific significance.

provisions in no way restrict the fair dealing exceptions granted for private study, academic
research, criticism, journalistic reporting or the communication of international quasi-judicial
proceedings before the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United

## 2. Official Graphic Charter

The visual identity of williamguindon.me reflects the balance between the institutional rigor of the
international legal file SEM-26-003 and the living preservation of the peat biodiversity of
Blainville.

### 2.1. System Color Palette

### 2.2. Typography

# Moderation Policy and Exchange Rules
Official multiplatform netiquette · In effect on all public spaces of William Guindon

### Rigorous, courteous and verifiable exchange framework
William Guindon's exchange spaces are public forums dedicated to scientific rigor, citizen
transparency and independent legal analysis. This policy aims to guarantee calm, secure and
constructive discussions for all participants.

## 1. Scope and mission

This moderation policy applies to all public exchange spaces managed by William Guindon, including:
- The official Facebook page (facebook.com/williamguindon.officiel ↗);
- The professional LinkedIn account (linkedin.com/in/william-guindon ↗);
- The YouTube channel (youtube.com/@william-guindon ↗);
- The official website (williamguindon.me).

These platforms are dedicated to sharing citizen initiatives, legal and environmental
popularization, as well as monitoring the SEM-26-003 communication filed with the Commission de
coopération environnementale (CCE). The goal is to foster rigorous, constructive and verifiable
public discussion.

## 2. Discussion principles

Technical questions, sharing of evidence, reasoned critiques, and respectfully expressed
disagreements are fully encouraged. Exchanges must remain:
- Courteous and respectful: Interventions must be addressed to the interlocutors with politeness,
-   without animosity;
- Focused on substance: The debates focus on documented facts, scientific studies, BAPE (Québec Bureau
-   of Public Environmental Hearings) data, environmental laws and decisions of CCE (Commission for
-   Environmental Cooperation);
- Sourced and verifiable: The provision of convincing and verifiable references is highly valued.

## 3. Prohibited behavior and content

To preserve everyone's safety and the holding of discussions, automated keyword filters as well as
active moderation are applied on each network. The following elements are systematically hidden or
deleted:

### 3.1. Violations of privacy and security (doxxing)
- Disclosure of private personal data (home addresses, personal numbers, educational institutions,
-   precise location);
- Threats, intimidation, stalking, stalking or inciting harassment online or offline.

### 3.2. Personal attacks, ageism and denigration
- Disparaging or condescending remarks targeting age, student status or personal background;
- Insults, vulgarities, hateful remarks, jeers and attacks of intent;
- Baseless allegations asserting a loss of independence (unfounded accusations of being a “puppet”, an
-   intermediary or delegating interventions).

### 3.3. Spam, scams and suspicious links
- External promotional links, commercial solicitations, cryptocurrencies or get-rich-quick offers;
- Questionable URL shorteners, redirects to private messengers or adult content;
- Inauthentic accounts, bot networks and fake profile farms.

### 3.4. Disinformation and partisan drift
- Defamatory comments, conspiracy theories or blatant fake news;
- Aggressive partisan co-opting aimed at diverting the issue toward political wrangling rather than
-   factual analysis of the laws.

## 4. Application, moderation and parental supervision

Any comments that violate these rules will be removed without notice.

On Facebook, LinkedIn and YouTube, repeat offenses or serious bullying behavior will result in
reporting to the host platform and permanent blocking of the account concerned.

Supervision and protection of a minor: In accordance with the policy relating to privacy and the
role of parents and under the legal duty of parental protection and assistance (art. 599 of Code
civil du Québec (Civil Code of Québec)), public exchange spaces, community moderation and monitoring
of communications on official networks are operated under the caring supervision of William's
parents. Any defamatory remarks, threats, acts of intimidation or inappropriate solicitation aimed
at a minor will be systematically reported to the platforms concerned and, if the seriousness
justifies it, to the competent authorities.

## 5. Contact and reporting

To report abusive content, ask a technical question or communicate confidentially with William
Guindon:

Authentic photos of the Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog), the terrain and

Saturday June 13, 2026 at 9:49 p.m.: start of writing the revised reply for CCE (ACEUM).
Cross-analysis of sections 35 and 36(3) of the Fisheries Act on the prohibition of destroying fish
habitat and discharging harmful substances, cross-checking of heavy metal analysis data (cadmium)
and satellite mapping.

Observations and photographic documentation in the field: wildlife remains suggesting acts of
poaching or unauthorized shooting, and wild deposits observed under Hydro-Québec rights of way in
the Plan-Bouchard sector. Contact details transmitted to the Protection of Quebec Wildlife
(MELCCFP).

During this bike ride, I headed towards the intermunicipal office to listen to see if any trucks
were circulating. I didn't hear any coming from the cell 6 area. Additionally, I was able to take in
the beautiful view of the water reflecting the clouds and watch firefighters perform tests.

Fence marking access to what appears to be a former National Defense sector at Plan-Bouchard. The
site remains inaccessible (padlocks sealed with wax), although a breach is visible on the other side
of the perimeter. The area could still harbor white phosphorous munitions residue. No hydrological
impact study seems to have assessed whether the pumping envisaged in cell 6 risks lowering the local
water table, while white phosphorus ignites spontaneously upon simple contact with air when it is
exposed.

Authentic aerial photograph showing the immediate proximity between the preserved natural wetland
and the industrial landfill site.

Precious ecosystem sheltering vulnerable species and playing a role as a major carbon sink for the
northern crown of Montreal.

William Guindon in Royal Canadian Army Cadet uniform, recipient of the Best Recruit award (2935
Bois-des-Filion Cadet Corps).

Official map of the Ville de Blainville (City of Blainville) demarcating the 550.0 hectares of
municipal natural environments of the Grande Tourbière and the 55.8 hectares acquired via the Green
and Blue Network (CMM Phase II), framing the industrial processing and landfill enclave of Stablex.

Official photograph of the natural environment of the Grande Tourbière de Blainville accompanying
the indigenous memory and environmental reconciliation file.

Morning light and plant strata of the Blainville ombrotrophic bog, a wetland of major ecological
importance for the metropolitan region.

# Registry & Hub of Integrity Policies and Charters
Exhaustive official directory of fundamental charters and legal documents governing citizen action
by William Guindon (SEM-26-003 CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) / ACEUM (Canada–United
States–Mexico Agreement)).

### Architecture of the Ethical and Legal Framework
In order to guarantee absolute clarity, flawless traceability and rigorous legal accessibility for
citizens, researchers, media and international bodies, the normative framework is split into
distinct and autonomous policies, supplemented by the legal conditions of use of the platform. Each
document has its own official page with its timestamp and delimited scope.

## The Fundamental Charters

### 01. Information Ethics & Media Rules
Commitments to documentary rigor, obligation of factual truth, ban on truncated quotes and protocol
of ethical requirements towards journalists.

### 02. Declaration of Independence & Integrity
Absolute political neutrality, non-partisan affiliation, self-financing of research, supervision of
conference fees, refusal of grants or partisan donations, and Formal public clarification notice
(A.5) on SEM-26-003.

### 03. AI Ethics & Human Control
Ethical supervision of AI models, exclusive human control over all background texts, integrity of
the training corpus, algorithmic transparency and formal legal exclusion of the AI ​​Documentary
Assistant.

### 04. Anti-SLAPP Statement & Legal Protection
Shield against SLAPP lawsuits (art. 51-54 C.P.C. Qc), right of international citizen participation
to the CCE (art. 24.27-24.28 ACEUM) and defense of environmental public interest debate.

### 05. Embargo Policy & Press Relations
Formal framework for early transmission of exclusive documents to accredited journalists and
sanctions in the event of embargo breaking.

### 06. Expert Consultative Framework & Scientific Rigor
Rules for occasional technical consultation with 16 scientists and lawyers, strict distinction
between consultative assistance and absence of co-signature.

### 07. Traceability of Evidence & Chain of Custody
Certified SHA-256 cryptographic fingerprints, immutable timestamp on the Bitcoin blockchain
(OpenTimestamps) and evidentiary custody chain.

### 08. Whistleblower Security (OpSec)
Operational security guidelines for secure and anonymous transmission of documents via Session,
Nostr and cryptographic channels.

### 09. Minor Citizen Status & Legal Guidelines
International citizen participation rights recognized for minors (United Nations Convention on the
Rights of the Child) and Quebec protective framework.

### 10. Privacy & Family Protection
Protection of the private sphere, family sanctuary and respect for autonomous communications by
William Guindon.

### 11. Accuracy & Media Relations Guidelines
Guidelines for factual integrity, transparency on non-exhaustive documentary selection and
preferences for equitable collaboration with information professionals.

### 12. Protection of the Published Documentary Archive
Evidentiary fixity, copyright compliance, legitimate reasons for redaction and formal withdrawal
procedure on Internet Archive and mirror platforms.

### 13. Correspondence, Archiving & Public Dissemination
Scope of correspondence (email, LinkedIn, Messenger), right to document capture and archiving,
commitment to complete anonymization of private exchanges and framework for public interest
dissemination of official communications.

## Associated Legal Documents

### License & Graphic Charter
Default CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 license policy, color palette, vector logos and official photo catalog.

### Terms of Use & License
Creative Commons CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 international license and platform usage rules.

### Privacy Policy
Compliance with Quebec Loi 25 (Québec privacy modernization Act) and GDPR, secure hosting without
intrusive tracking.

### Netiquette & Moderation
Courtesy rules and moderation policy on official social networks.

### Credits, Licenses & Third Parties
Comprehensive inventory of software dependencies, cryptographic libraries and third-party licenses.

### Legal Contact & Reports
For any questions relating to these charters, material reporting or request for legal clarification:

PAGE 37/50 · https://williamguindon.me/portrait.html
Title : Portrait: Who is William Guindon, the young activist who challenges governments on the Stablex issue?
==============================================================================

[Caption] PHOTO PATRICK SANFAÇON, LA PRESSE William Guindon, 15 years old, author of the
international citizen communication SEM-26-003 filed with the Commission de coopération
environnementale (CCE / ACEUM).

William Guindon, born August 3, 2011 in Sainte-Marthe-sur-le-Lac, is a Quebec environmental
activist. He is the author of citizen communication SEM-26-003, filed on May 1, 2026 as part of the
Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement (ACEUM), aimed at curbing the expansion of the Stablex
hazardous waste landfill in Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog). He was
fourteen years old at the time of filing. He has fifteen since August 3, 2026.

This text was first published on July 4, 2026, when the file was taking off. Since then, almost
everything has changed: Canada responded, the author replied, an unprecedented certificate was
issued, a federal petition was cataloged, a public register delivered its first lines, and Quebec
changed government.

Here's what happened — and what it changes in the picture.

## What has happened since July

### October 16: Ottawa responds without responding
On October 16, 2026, the deadline set by the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation)
Secretariat, the Government of Canada files its official response, Exhibit 72-RSP. The Secretariat
acknowledges receipt the same day, document 73-REC, under the signature of its director of legal
affairs, Paolo Solano.

The federal brief is two pages long.

It does not contain any challenge to the material findings of the file: neither the cadmium
discharges measured up to 320 times the criterion for the protection of aquatic life, nor the
destruction of 278,000 square meters of wetlands, nor the absence of any prior federal
authorization. It does not report any enforcement measures of federal law relating to cell 6 of the
Stablex site.

Its strategy lies in a procedural dismissal. Canada requests the Secretariat to terminate
consideration of the communication, or to suspend its processing, invoking Article 24.27(4)(a) of
the ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement) and Guideline 7.5. In his eyes, environmental
petition No. 0546 filed with the Office of the Auditor General of Canada would be an internal
procedure still pending, which would make citizen communication premature.

In other words: the State invokes its own administrative deadlines to request the closure of a file
which documents its inaction.

### October 17: Seven means in twenty-four hours
The next day, William Guindon submitted a memorandum of observations to the Secretariat which
refuted Ottawa's request point by point. Seven arguments, based on the jurisprudence of the
mechanism.
- First means: a petition to the Auditor General is neither a judicial procedure, nor an
-   administrative procedure initiated by the Party, nor a quasi-judicial procedure. The Office of the
-   Auditor General has no sanctioning powers and does not verify the accuracy of ministerial
-   responses. It cannot therefore constitute the domestic remedy which Article 24.27(4)(a) requires
-   to be exhausted.
- Second ground: Guideline 7.5 exists to protect the citizen's recourse against external interference,
-   not to provide an escape route for the state in question.
- Third ground: the Secretariat had already ruled on this question. Paragraphs 66 to 79 of the August
-   17 decision expressly address the coexistence of approaches, relying on the Tijuana, Nazas and
-   Ship Pollution jurisprudence. Going back on it would constitute an unjustified reversal.
- Fourth ground: the two approaches have neither the same nature nor the same scope. The review before
-   the CCE concerns the application of seventeen provisions from five federal environmental laws —
-   the Migratory Birds Convention Act, the Migratory Birds Regulations, the Species at Risk Act, the
-   Fisheries Act and the Canadian Environmental Protection Act. Petition 0546 only concerns two laws.
- Fifth means: requiring the prior exhaustion of simple questions of ministerial information would
-   create a dangerous precedent for all citizens of the three signatory countries of the trade
-   agreement.
- Sixth ground: the requested postponement is by nature indeterminate, since article 24.28 of the
-   ACEUM does not provide for any reopening mechanism once an examination has been suspended.
- Seventh ground: on the merits, Canada's response does not mention any concrete measure of
-   application of federal environmental law on the site of cell 6. The alleged lack of application is
-   therefore not contested.

Conclusion requested from the Secretariat: reject the request to terminate the examination, reject
the request for suspension, and continue the investigation with a view to recommending to the
Council of Ministers of Canada, the United States and Mexico the development of a complete factual
record.

## A first in thirty-two years

On September 25, 2026, the CCE Enforcement Issues Submissions Unit confirmed in writing a fact that
goes beyond the scope of the local file:

The clarification deserves to be cited as is. The CCE does not compile the age of the authors: it
never had to do so. In thirty-two years of existence of the ANACE mechanism then of the ACEUM, and
through more than a hundred communications examined in Canada, the United States and Mexico, no
citizen under the age of eighteen had crossed the threshold of admissibility.

The day before, September 24, William Guindon had published a clarification notice confirming his
status as sole author and his absolute independence. The file was designed, written and carried by a
teenager. Not by an adult agent who would lend his name, nor by an organization which would finance
covertly. The documents are signed by his hand, with the countersignature of his legal
representative as required by Quebec civil law.

## One hundred and nineteen days without a response

On June 29, 2026, he submitted an environmental petition to the Office of the Auditor General of
Canada. The Commissioner of the Environment and Sustainable Development registers it under number
15-0546. The federal public catalog shows a first inscription:

The petition contains twenty-four questions for ministerial information. Ten of them relate to
cadmium. The file cites surface water analyzes carried out along the Grande Tourbière, revealing
concentrations reaching 320 times the criterion for protecting aquatic life.

On July 13, federal departments formally received the petition, which triggered the legal response
deadline of 120 days set by section 22 of the Auditor General Act.

On October 9, seven days before the international deadline, he published a press release to
underline a parallel countdown: 119 days had passed since the transmission to the ministries without
any response being provided to the questions on cadmium. The federal legal deadline expires on
November 10, 2026.

## Quebec has changed government

The issue has also changed political context.

On October 5, 2026, the Parti Québécois formed a minority government. Paul St-Pierre Plamondon was
sworn in as prime minister on October 21, succeeding Christine Fréchette. At Environment, Jean
Pellerin succeeds Pascale Déry.

In Blainville, the constituency changed its face: Nadine Le Gal, of the Parti Québécois, won with
33.69% of the votes and 14,999 votes, ahead of Sébastien Daoust, of the Liberal Party, at 26.55%,
and Pascal Quevillon, of the Conservative Party, at 15.97%. The outgoing CAQ deputy, Mario
Laframboise, finished fourth. On October 21, Nadine Le Gal was named president of the government
caucus.

Two parliamentary meetings are confirmed for November: with her, who requested the exchange even
before the opening of her constituency office, and with Madeleine Chenette, liberal federal MP for
Thérèse-De Blainville and parliamentary secretary. On the program: Loi 93 (Bill 93), report 371 of
BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings), the federal response of November 10 and the
rest of the procedure before CCE.

## Who is William Guindon

William Guindon spent his childhood in Sainte-Marthe-sur-le-Lac, in the Laurentides region of
Quebec. He completed his primary studies in Sainte-Marthe-sur-le-Lac, a neighboring municipality.
From the sixth grade, he showed a keen interest in science and the environment by winning third
place in the regional final of the Expo-Sciences Laurentides.

He then continued his secondary school career at Externat Sacré-Cœur, a private establishment
located in Rosemère. His skills and involvement allow him to distinguish himself on several
occasions. In 2023, he received the Méritas Prize in the “ambassador — intellectual curiosity”
category, followed in 2024 by the Méritas Prize “Engagement and humanism”. His excellent academic
results also allowed him to obtain an exemption from the French and science final exams. Alongside
his studies, he got involved in the Royal Canadian Army Cadets, where he reached the silver star
level.

His involvement extends well beyond the academic framework. From the age of ten, he chose to
volunteer in his community. From June to September 2022, then again from June 2023, he offers his
time to the leisure teams of two residential and long-term care centers (CHSLD) in the Laval region.
Between 2023 and 2024, he will coordinate the community garden of the Externat Sacré-Cœur, carried
out in close collaboration with the City of Rosemère.

## A journey marked by brilliant actions

### 2021: First public attention
On November 28, 2021, at the age of ten, William Guindon attracted media attention by becoming one
of the very first Quebec children in the 5 to 11 age group to receive the COVID-19 vaccine. This
symbolic and civic gesture earned him public congratulations from the Prime Minister of Quebec,
François Legault.

### 2025: Political questioning in Le Devoir
A few years later, his fight shifted to political and environmental terrain. In 2025, he published
an open letter in the daily Le Devoir, entitled “François Legault, you are destroying our future”.
He directly challenges the head of the provincial government on the environmental and
intergenerational consequences resulting from the Stablex expansion project.

This letter appears the day after the adoption under gag order of Projet de loi 93 (Bill 93).

### May 1, 2026: The international legal offensive
On May 1, 2026, William Guindon took a major step forward by bringing the issue to the international
stage. He files an official communication, registered under the reference SEM-26-003, with the
Commission de coopération environnementale (Commission for Environmental Cooperation), the
monitoring body created by the ACEUM trade agreement.

In this document, he argues that Canada is failing in its international legal obligations regarding
the protection of migratory birds, species at risk and the quality of surface waters. The cause of
this failure: the authorization to expand the Stablex industrial site in the heart of a sensitive
ecological zone.

The CCE acknowledges receipt of the complaint on May 4, 2026 and begins an in-depth examination of
the file under the articles of the cross-border agreement.

On June 3, 2026, the Secretariat concluded that the communication did not yet meet all the criteria,
but invited the author to submit a revised communication. This is what he will do, six weeks later.

### May 2026: The urgent appeal to the United Nations
At the same time as his approach to the CCE, he contacted the United Nations Special Rapporteur on
toxic substances and human rights, Marcos Orellana.

In this appeal, he documents the environmental and health risk represented by the burial of
dangerous materials in a humid environment, emphasizing that this type of project infringes on the
rights of present and future generations.

### July 2026: Intervention at the Blainville municipal council
On July 2, 2026, William Guindon spoke publicly during the Blainville municipal council meeting.

He confronts Mayor Liza Poulin about the ongoing procedures and the City's transparency in this
matter.

He officially filed several key documents, including his complaint to the CCE and the United
Nations, as well as an official letter addressed to the Federal Minister of Foreign Affairs, Mélanie
Joly.

On July 7, he sent a formal notice to the mayor and members of the municipal council, demanding the
public online posting without delay of all the documents tabled during the July 2 meeting.

The City complied: on July 9, it published on its official website a public notice confirming the
submission of communication SEM-26-003 and made all the documents accessible.

This municipal deposition led to regional and national media coverage: the TVBL report, broadcast
the same evening of July 9, presented in detail the intervention of the young activist.

### July 2026: The alliance with the Longhouse
A few days later, he obtained institutional support of a different nature: Longhouse Stuart Myiow,
traditional custodian of the Kanien'kehá:ka longhouse, gave him official support under the aegis of
the Two-Way Wampum.

This alliance links modern legal approach to the age-old protection of the territory by indigenous
peoples. It marks a turning point in citizen mobilization by placing the fight for Grande Tourbière
in a perspective of environmental justice and real reconciliation.

### July 16, 2026: The revised submission
On July 16, 2026, William Guindon submitted his revised communication to the Secretariat of the CCE.

This is no longer a teenage complaint. It is a 45-page legal brief, structured in six sections,
accompanied by 222 evidentiary documents, with detailed inventories, chain of custody, forensic
metadata and article-by-article concordance.

The entire file is preserved on Zenodo, CERN's open archive, under the identifier DOI
10.5281/zenodo.21416989.

The author demonstrates how Canada fails to apply five separate federal environmental laws in
connection with the Stablex cell 6 project.

### August 1, 2026: UNESCO and Committee on the Rights of the Child
On August 1, 2026, he further broadened his international offensive by submitting a joint brief to
the UNESCO World Heritage Committee and the United Nations Committee on the Rights of the Child.

It argues that the destruction of a thousand-year-old peatland and the exposure of ecosystems to
persistent contaminants violate the rights guaranteed by the Convention on the Rights of the Child,
in particular the right to a healthy environment, health and development.

### August 17, 2026: The decision of the CCE
On August 17, 2026, the Commission de coopération environnementale Secretariat issues its decision
under Article 24.27(2) and (3) of the ACEUM, document 26-2-DET2.

The CCE concludes that the revised communication meets all admissibility criteria and that it
warrants a response from the Government of Canada.

The Secretariat formally directs Canada to provide a detailed written response by October 16, 2026.

The decision made headlines. This is the first time in the history of the trade agreement that the
complaint of a minor citizen forces a signatory state to explain itself.

## The bog before the bog

We are talking about an industrial site and a wetland. We rarely talk about what was there before.

On September 25, 2026, William Guindon published an independent documentary file which places the
Grande Tourbière in a much longer temporality: six thousand years of organic memory, the history of
the Kanien'kehá:ka, Anishinaabeg and Haudenosaunee peoples, and the water territories of the Mille
Îles River, the Makigôtekw. The file also recalls that this territory was never ceded.

It is not a cultural supplement. It is a change of legal and moral framework. A peat bog is not a
wasteland that is replaced by a technical work: it is a carbon sink that took millennia to form, a
water reservoir, a habitat and a place of memory. The BAPE said it in his own way in 2023,
describing the project as premature. The indigenous file adds a voice which, in Quebec, has long
been kept away from these decisions.

The link with the rest of the fight is not theoretical: in July 2026, the support of Longhouse
Stuart Myiow, obtained under the aegis of the two-way Wampum, is exactly in line with this
continuity. Moral support — and, if necessary, support in court.

## The Stablex file: the underside of an environmental confrontation

The Grande Tourbière de Blainville is a unique millennial ecosystem in Quebec. The Stablex Canada
site has been in operation there since 1983. It is the only industrial center authorized in the
province for the final burial of inorganic industrial residues previously stabilized and solidified.

The conflict escalated in March 2025, when the Quebec government chose to adopt Projet de loi 93
under an accelerated gag procedure. This law forces the municipality of Blainville to transfer land
of great ecological value in order to allow the development of the company's new landfill zone,
called “cell 6”.

This political decision was imposed despite a very unfavorable opinion from Bureau d'audiences
publiques sur l'environnement (BAPE), published in September 2023 (Report 371), which then described
the expansion project as “premature”. To protect the company, the law adopted by Quebec includes
strict clauses excluding almost any possibility of citizen legal recourse on the merits of the case.
Legal organizations, such as the Centre québécois du droit de l'environnement (CQDE), denounce this
measure which they consider to be a serious attack on the right to an effective legal remedy.

Work on fitting out the cell has begun. The operating permit had still not been issued in the summer
of 2026: the Ministry of the Environment then indicated that the authorization request was “still
under analysis”. The matter is now the responsibility of a new government.

Citizen mobilization is also based on environmental data. Water tests conducted by citizen groups in
December 2023 reveal concentrations of cadmium in nearby surface waters up to 320 times higher than
current environmental standards. A second independent campaign, conducted in June 2026 by Climat
Québec and the Blainville Citizens' Coalition and supervised by limnologist Sonja Behmel, of
WaterShed Monitoring, also documents the presence of zinc, copper, arsenic and chromium around the
site. Stablex contests these conclusions and maintains that its most recent ministerial sampling
campaigns, published in February 2026, conclude that its activities are compliant.

Finally, opponents point out that nearly 41% of the hazardous materials buried in Blainville come
from outside Quebec, including approximately 29% directly from the United States.

## An investigation into the Registry of Lobbyists

On October 24, 2026, William Guindon published a public file constructed from a single source: the
Registry of Lobbyists of Canada. No revelation, no confidential document. Registrations, numbers,
dates, verifiable by anyone.

The register contains a registration opened on March 26, 2026, number 964373-385380, version 1.
Type: consultant lobbyist. Client: Stablex, 760 boulevard Industriel, in Blainville, represented by
its general manager, Michel Perron. Lobbyist: Alexandra Boudreault, of Catapulte communication, with
no prior public office declared. Reported parent company: Republic Services, Massena, New York. The
declared purpose, quoted in full: “Advise and inform civil servants and elected officials on the
management of residual hazardous materials at Stablex at the federal level, in addition to keeping
them informed of the work on cell 6.” Institutions targeted: Environment and Climate Change Canada
and the House of Commons.

The register also contains a monthly communication report, number 385380-701620. Communication:
September 17, 2026. Posting: September 17, 2026, the same day.

The designated public office holders met that day were three officials from Environment and Climate
Change Canada: Michael Bonser, assistant deputy minister of the International Affairs Branch; Ailish
Campbell, Senior Assistant Deputy Minister; and Mylène Sauvé, Director of Multilateral Affairs.

This is where the wires cross. Michael Bonser is not just the assistant deputy minister of a branch
of ECCC. He is the official representative of Canada to the Commission de coopération
environnementale: the one whose name appears on the liaison documents with the Secretariat, the one
whose services coordinated the drafting of the official response due on October 16.

A month separates the September 17 meeting from Ottawa's October 16 response. The two facts are in
separate public records. One at the Office of the Commissioner of Lobbying of Canada, the other at
the communications registry of the CCE. The file published on October 24 simply puts them side by
side.

The register finally shows a formal anomaly, which the file is content to document. For this
specific registration, the Registry of Lobbyists of Canada only displays one monthly communication
report — that of September 17 — while the “Reporting period” field displays “August 2026”. A
communication held in September declared over a period of August: a typo of entry or an
administrative catch-up, but a trace that the register keeps as is.

You have to be precise about what this file says and does not say. He cites public records. It
assumes no intention, alleges no collusion and asserts no causal link. The Register of Lobbyists is
a register of transparency: lobbyists declare their activities there because the law obliges them to
do so, and the fact of declaring a meeting does not constitute an offense. What the file shows is
the temporal concomitance between a lobbying activity targeting the very management that coordinated
Canada's response and the filing of a procedural dismissal four weeks later.

## A precedent for minors

This issue is not only environmental. It raises a legal question that goes beyond Grande Tourbière:
that of direct access for minors to international justice.

On September 23, 2026, William Guindon published a plea on the legal autonomy of adolescents in the
face of the structural adultism of Quebec and Canadian law. Its central argument: biological age
cannot be an automatic inadmissibility criterion when a citizen demonstrates his capacity for
discernment and provides rigorous proof.

On October 14, 2026, in Berlin, this plea received scientific support. Manfred Liebel, professor
emeritus at the Technical University of Berlin and global figure for children's rights, signs a
declaration in support of the approach:

The practical significance is there: a file of two hundred and twenty-two evidentiary documents,
filed without a lawyer, which forces the federal government of Canada to mobilize its legal teams
and produce an official response in an international treaty. If a fifteen-year-old can do it, the
age barrier is no longer a procedural inevitability.

It is also in this light that the rest of the work must be read: the full-text search engine and the
downloadable ZIP archive put online on September 22, which make the documents accessible to all
citizens without an intermediary; the ethical charters published on its site; the parts traceability
register. The entire architecture of the site was designed for others to use.

## Democratizing environmental justice

Making your voice heard shouldn't be an obstacle course.

After having passed the procedural requirements of the Commission de coopération environnementale,
the young activist refuses to allow his apprenticeship to remain an isolated experience.

Refusing to remain a simple observer, William Guindon launched the writing of a practical and
popularized guide for citizens and young people wishing to understand the workings of international
law and navigate complex legal processes.

Objectives: decode the legislative framework and Canadian and international environmental
regulations, popularize the steps necessary to submit an admissible communication, and share the
lessons learned from one's own journey with ministries and the courts.

## Media appearances

The impact of William Guindon's legal and international efforts has generated notable media
attention, locally, nationally and internationally.

On June 11, 2026, journalist Éanna Mackey published an investigative report in the independent
investigative media The Rover, entitled “Blainville teenager takes Stablex fight international”. The
article details William Guindon's journey from his discovery of the file to the filing of his
complaint before the CCE, and analyzes the flaws in the regulatory framework surrounding the
management of dangerous materials in Blainville.

On July 3, 2026, La Presse devoted a major report, signed by journalist Katia Gagnon and illustrated
by photographs by Patrick Sanfaçon, entitled “The fight of a teenager at the UN”. This major report
highlights the determination of the young activist, his simultaneous recourse to the CCE and the
United Nations, and the legal significance of his approach to the protection of Quebec wetlands.

On Thursday, July 9, 2026 at 7 p.m., William Guindon reached a new media milestone by being the main
guest of an exclusive 11-minute interview on the regional news show Le Télé-Journal on TVBL, hosted
by journalist Pierre-Luc Paquin.

The interview allowed the young activist to explain directly to the citizens of the Lower
Laurentians the reasons for his intervention before the Blainville municipal council, the details of
his international communication SEM-26-003, and the reasons for his refusal to give up in the face
of Loi 93.

On June 16, 2026, William Guindon was the subject of a detailed portrait in the youth media Les as
de l'info, entitled “William Guindon: a 14-year-old activist who defies borders”.

This platform allowed him to directly appeal to the school-aged public, to explain his 250 hours of
documentary research, and to concretely demonstrate that young people are not condemned to passive
eco-anxiety: they can seize the tools of the law to actively defend the environment.

On July 23, 2026, William Guindon was the subject of a full 23-minute episode on the CBC national
podcast, This is Montreal, hosted by Antoni Nercessian, entitled "The 14-year-old fighting a
hazardous waste site".

This forum allowed him to present on a national and international scale the issues surrounding
Projet de loi 93, the importation of American hazardous waste and the protection of Grande Tourbière
de Blainville.

On August 5, 2026, the legal information organization Éducaloi published an in-depth analysis
entitled “Filing a complaint internationally to enforce environmental laws: the case of a
14-year-old teenager”.

The article dissects the legal mechanism of article 24.27 of ACEUM, explains why the appeal of an
adolescent before an international commission constitutes a first, and analyzes the conditions which
make such a complaint admissible before the signatory governments.

In September 2026, William Guindon took part in the filming of a major documentary project led by
Lab22 (laboratory for social and environmental innovations in schools, in partnership with the Cégep
du Vieux-Montréal).

The filming, carried out on the shores of Lacs Fauvel and along the Grande Tourbière de Blainville,
highlights the journey of young Quebec environmental leaders who use innovative institutional, legal
and scientific levers to protect their living environment.

The first broadcast of the documentary is planned for 2027, before official distribution and
screenings in schools and environmental film festivals across Quebec.

## What's coming

November 10, 2026 — Legal deadline: The ministers of Environment and Climate Change Canada,
Fisheries and Oceans Canada and Health Canada must table in Parliament their written responses to
the twenty-four questions in environmental petition no. 15-0546.

November 2026 — Two parliamentary meetings: Nadine Le Gal, MP for Blainville and president of the
government caucus, and Madeleine Chenette, federal MP for Thérèse-De Blainville.

November 12 and 13, 2026: Conference of the ACTES Network of the Centrale des syndicats du Québec
(CSQ), in Mauricie.

November 27, 2026: Green day of the AREQ-CSQ Laurentides, in Saint-Jérôme, where he will deliver the
conference “Transmitting commitment”.

Around December 15, 2026 — Decision CCE: End of the sixty working day period for the examination
opened under article 24.28 of ACEUM: the Secretariat of CCE will indicate whether or not it
recommends the creation of an independent factual file.

December 2026: Submission of the report from the environment commission of the Metropolitan
Community of Montreal (CMM) on the management of residual hazardous materials.

2027: If a factual record is recommended, the decision rests with the environment ministers of
Canada, the United States and Mexico.

In the meantime, at the municipal level: The new Stablex Coordination Committee set up by the Ville
de Blainville (City of Blainville) began its work in October 2026, and Quebec paid compensation of
$17 million to the City in August for the loss of value of the land transferred.

#### Academic Evidence: International Scientific Support Statement (Berlin) — Civic Capacity & Structural Adultism
Official statement signed in Berlin by Prof. Dr. Manfred Liebel (Emeritus Professor of Sociology at
the Technische Universität Berlin) and Philip Meade, M.A. (Fachhochschule Potsdam / Alice Salomon
Hochschule Berlin), co-authors of the reference work Adultism: A Critical Introduction to
Discrimination Against Children (2026). They certify the quality of William Guindon's civic approach
and qualify the administrative refusals suffered as “typical examples of structural adultism”.
> “From the perspective of the sociology of childhood and research on children's rights, civic and
> analytical capacity is not acquired by magic on the day of the 18th birthday: it is demonstrated in
> practice through concrete agency. [...] I therefore fully support the approach of petition e-7834 to
> the House of Commons of Canada [...]. » — Prof. Dr. Manfred Liebel & Philip Meade, M.A. (Berlin,
> October 14, 2026)

version of the communication SEM-26-003. On screen: in-depth analysis of sections 35 and 36(3) of
the Fisheries Act (formal prohibition on the deterioration of fish habitat and the discharge of

standards of file integrity for one or more of the following formal reasons:

The content has not been authorized, reviewed or validated by William Guindon.

The media outlet failed to arrange any prior interviews, consult the author, or refuse a fair right
of reply.

The journalistic angle betrays the reality of the approach (e.g.: purely anecdotal framing instead
of factual and legal rigor).

The information has been modified, invented, or the comments have been distorted or taken out of
context.

Marked use of ageism or institutional paternalism, infantilizing a leading international legal and
scientific procedure.

The publication contains material deficiencies, erroneous figures or inaccurate legal statements
that have never been corrected.
