# Privacy Policy
Last legal update in effect: September 6, 2026 (September 6, 2026)

### Commitment to legal and ethical compliance
This Privacy Policy establishes in a transparent, factual and exhaustive manner the rules governing
the collection, use, retention, protection and destruction of personal information on the
williamguindon.me site. It is established in strict compliance with the Act respecting the
protection of personal information in the private sector of Quebec (CQLR, c. P-39.1, amended by Loi
25 (Québec privacy modernization Act)), the Personal Information Protection and Electronic Documents
Act (PIPEDA / PIPEDA) of Canada, and aligns with the standards of excellence of the General Data
Protection Regulation (GDPR / GDPR) of the European Union.

## 1. Identification of the Person Responsible for the Protection of Personal Information

In accordance with section 3.2 of the Act respecting the protection of personal information in the
private sector of Quebec (Loi 25), the person assuming the highest authority and legal
responsibility for the protection of personal information is:
- Legal representative: William Guindon
- Status: Author and submitter of citizen communication SEM-26-003 (Commission de coopération
-   environnementale / ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement))
- Location: Blainville, Province of Quebec, Canada
- Web portal: https://williamguindon.me/
- Secure direct contact channel: ACCEssible via the cryptographic button at the bottom of the page or
-   via Client-Side Nostr Secure Messaging.

## 2. Fundamentals of Processing and Zero Profiling

The site applies the cardinal principles of Privacy by Design and Privacy by Default:
- Absolute minimization: No personal data is collected without an explicit, legitimate and
-   proportionate purpose.
- No commercialization: Personal information is never sold, rented, exchanged, monetized or
-   transferred to third parties for advertising or commercial purposes.
- Absence of automated decision-making: No profiling, discriminatory automated decision-making or
-   behavioral evaluation system is implemented on this site.

## 3. Data Collected and Processing Methods

### 3.1. General navigation and strict policy of no advertising cookies (Zero-Cookie Policy)
The site does not install any advertising cookies, any cross-site trackers, any invisible pixels or
any commercial retargeting scripts.
- Local display preferences: The choice of display mode (dark or light theme) is stored exclusively in
-   the local memory of your own web browser (localStorage). This purely technical information is kept
-   on your device, is not associated with any personal identity and is never transmitted to our
-   servers.
- Functional translation cookie (Google Translate): The Google translation module can place a
-   functional cookie (googtrans) in order to memorize the language chosen during your browsing
-   session. This cookie is not advertising and is not used for profiling purposes.
- Privacy-friendly audience measurement (Umami Analytics): The site uses the open-source statistical
-   analysis solution Umami Analytics (hosted on cloud.umami.is). Unlike invasive analysis tools,
-   Umami works without any connection cookies (cookieless), does not collect any data allowing the
-   identification of an individual (PII), completely anonymizes IP addresses by daily cryptographic
-   hashing with salt, and does not track any user from one site to another. This mechanism strictly
-   complies with the directives of the CNIL, the Loi 25 and the GDPR and does not require any
-   intrusive consent by banner.

### 3.2. Voluntary correspondence and contact form
When you voluntarily communicate with William Guindon (by email or secure form), the following data
may be processed:
- First and last name (as provided);
- Email address;
- Purpose and content of your message, report, interview request or civic communication.

Exclusive purpose: Respond to your correspondence, ensure civic, scientific, media or documentary
monitoring related to matters of public interest processed on this site.

### 3.3. Absence of commercial newsletter and monitoring by free syndication (RSS / Atom feed)
In order to preserve the complete independence of the citizen approach and to eliminate any risk of
exposure of personal data, this site does not manage any email newsletters and does not maintain any
subscriber contact database. No mass email subcontractor (such as Brevo) is active on the site.
- Open and decentralized distribution: All citizen news, official press releases and procedural
-   updates relating to the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) SEM-26-003 file are
-   publicly distributed via the standardized RSS / Atom feed (feed.xml).
- Zero collection and zero profiling: RSS syndication is read directly by your own reader or news
-   aggregator, without prior registration, without transmission of contact address and without
-   connection cookies.
- Purge of previous data: No historical mailing lists are retained or used. If you had previously
-   expressed your interest by email, your contact details have been purged from the automated
-   modules.

## 4. Secure Messaging, Client-Side Encryption and Source Protection (Nostr / NIP-59 / NIP-44)

As part of the protection of journalistic sources, whistleblowers and engaged citizens, the site
integrates a cutting-edge communication module based on the decentralized Nostr (Secure Messaging)
protocol governed by the following technical guarantees:
- Zero-Knowledge Architecture: All cryptographic operations are executed exclusively on the client
-   side (in the RAM of your browser) via the standardized Web Crypto API. No private keys, no clear
-   messages and no decryption keys pass through or are hosted on williamguindon.me servers.
- Triple Cryptographic Envelope (NIP-59 Gift Wrap & NIP-17): The initial message (*Rumor*, kind 14) is
-   encrypted and sealed (*Seal*, kind 13), then inserted into a disposable wrapper (*Gift Wrap*, kind
-   1059) signed by a unique random ephemeral key. Decentralized network relays cannot technically
-   know the identity of the sender, the recipient, or the actual transmission timestamp.
- NIP-44 v2 encryption and protection against traffic analysis: The encryption combines ECDH on
-   secp256k1 curve, HKDF-SHA256, AES-GCM 256 bits and a geometric padding algorithm, preventing any
-   spying by analyzing the size of network packets.
- Encrypted files and attachments: Any attached document or photograph is encrypted locally in RAM in
-   AES-GCM 256 bits before distribution. The symmetric decryption key is sealed inside the encrypted
-   envelope NIP-59.
- Ephemerality and irreversible purge (NIP-40): The user can assign a programmable self-destruction
-   duration to his message, ordering its definitive deletion from all network relays.
- Right to immediate deletion on the device: A complete purge button (“Erase everything from this
-   device”) allows the user to instantly destroy their local keys in RAM memory, their caches and any
-   trace of session.
- Total absence of obligation and full discharge: Sending an encrypted message does not confer any
-   mandate, establishes any professional or legal relationship, and in no way commits William Guindon
-   to respond, publish, disclose or process the transmitted elements. The sender remains solely
-   responsible for his transmissions and the safekeeping of his private keys.

### 4.1. Secure Transmission of Large Files and Voluminous Folders (SwissTransfer / Infomaniak)
For sending evidentiary documents, technical archives or recordings exceeding the limit of email
attachments (up to 50 GB per transfer, free and without registration), the SwissTransfer service
(operated by Infomaniak Network SA in Switzerland) is recommended under the following legal and
technical guarantees:
- 1. Intellectual property and rights to data: Infomaniak does not grant itself any rights to your
-   files. The content is never analyzed, never resold, and never used to train artificial
-   intelligence models.
- 2. Ephemerality and permanent deletion: The files are stored strictly temporarily (up to 30 days
-   maximum at the sender's choice) and are permanently and irreversibly deleted from the servers as
-   soon as the link expires or after downloading.
- 3. Double encryption in transit and at rest: Transfers circulate via an encrypted HTTPS / AES-GCM
-   protocol (secure anti-interception tunnel) and benefit from double hardware and software locking
-   at rest (LUKS and AES 256 bits). The sender can also lock access with a personalized password.
- 4. Swiss sovereignty and protective legal framework: The infrastructure is 100% Swiss, operated in
-   Infomaniak's proprietary data centers, without any subcontracting to American hyperscalers. The
-   data is governed by the Federal Data Protection Act (Swiss DPA) and the European GDPR. In the
-   absence of an American parent company, the infrastructure is not subject to direct
-   extraterritorial injunction mechanisms such as the American CLOUD Act.
- Technical transparency: Encryption keys are managed server-side to enable direct web distribution
-   without requiring the installation of third-party software at the recipient.

## 5. AI Virtual Assistance Module (Cloudflare Workers & Groq)

The site provides a citizen conversational assistant to facilitate access and understanding of
public environmental documentation (folder CCE SEM-26-003). The processing is governed by the
following rules:
- Secure Serverless Proxy (Cloudflare Workers): Requests to the helper are routed through a serverless
-   function (Serverless Edge Worker) running on Cloudflare's edge network. This proxy completely
-   isolates the inference secret key, filters requests, and enforces strict guardrails without ever
-   storing content on a persistent server.
- Transient and anonymous processing: The questions asked and a contextual extract from the public
-   documentation are transmitted in real time by the Worker to the Groq inference engine. No user
-   account, cookie or personal identifier is required or transmitted.
- Persistent lack of logging: The site editor does not keep any logs of conversations, any chat
-   history or any log of prompts submitted to the assistant.
- Non-use for training: The data from your queries is in no way used by the publisher to train or
-   refine artificial intelligence models.
- Absence of automated decision (Loi 25, art. 12.1): The assistance module is a purely informative and
-   documentary tool. It does not render any automated decision producing legal effects or affecting
-   in any way the persons concerned.
- Technical retention of the Groq processor: Requests passing through the Groq Inc. API are processed
-   in RAM. In accordance with Groq's corporate policy for its API inference services, data is
-   retained for a maximum technical period of 30 days for security and abuse detection purposes only,
-   with no reuse for model training.
- No Advice and Disclaimer: AI responses are generated probabilistically and automatically. They do
-   not in any way constitute a promise, legal advice, expert advice or a formal position taken by the
-   publisher.
- Caution: Users are strongly recommended not to enter any confidential or sensitive personal
-   information into the AI ​​chat interface.

## 6. Duration of Retention of Personal Information

Personal information is kept only for the period necessary to fulfill the purposes for which it was
collected, in accordance with legal requirements:
- Email correspondence and citizen requests: Kept for a maximum period of 24 months from the last
-   exchange to ensure documentary monitoring and legal obligations, unless expressly requested for
-   early deletion.
- Technical logs and navigation without profiling: The publisher does not carry out any individualized
-   tracking or navigation profiling. No correlation of IP addresses with any identity is made. At the
-   level of the network infrastructure (Cloudflare, GitHub Pages), IP addresses pass through and are
-   processed in RAM for strict reasons of security, routing and attack mitigation (legitimate
-   security interest, art. 6.1.f GDPR / Loi 25) according to the respective policies of these hosts.

## 7. Data Communication, Third Party Processors and Network Infrastructure

Your personal information is not communicated to any third party, with the strict exception of
technical and infrastructure providers essential to the secure, resilient and efficient operation of
the website:
- The domain is protected and distributed via the global network of . As such, Cloudflare acts as a
-   reverse proxy ( ) and content delivery network (CDN):
- GitHub Pages / GitHub Inc.: Hosting of source code and public static files under SOC 1 / SOC 2
-   certified infrastructure.
- Google LLC (Google Translation — translate.google.com / translate.googleapis.com): Optional
-   third-party multilingual automatic translation service activated at the visitor's request. Using
-   the selector loads translation scripts hosted by Google and passes the text to be translated.
- Cal.com, Inc. ( app.cal.com ): Optionally integrated appointment scheduling platform (press area and
-   media contact). When the visitor interacts with the calendar, Cal.com collects the entered contact
-   information necessary to book the interview.
- unpkg.com (CDN Content Delivery Network): One-time loading of external sub-resources (including the
-   third-party carbon badge script licensed under GPL-3.0 with SRI integrity checking, as well as the
-   cmaps font mapping files needed to render PDF documents).
- Umami Analytics (cloud.umami.is): Open-source audience statistics solution, without individual
-   tracker or persistent cookie, respecting privacy and Loi 25 / GDPR directives.
- Meta Platforms, Inc. (Official Facebook page) & Google LLC (YouTube channel via
-   youtube-nocookie.com): The site includes official broadcast windows (iframes) from Meta Platforms
-   (Facebook page) and Google LLC (YouTube channel via youtube-nocookie.com). These windows allow
-   direct consultation of public declarations, documentary videos and citizen interventions. Although
-   the site editor does not retain any data relating to this consultation, these third-party
-   platforms may process network metrics (IP addresses) and cookies in accordance with their
-   respective privacy policies when the user interacts with these components.
- ImageKit (ImageKit India Pvt. Ltd. / ik.imagekit.io): A media content delivery network (CDN) and
-   image and video optimization engine used to accelerate the seamless delivery of field photographs,
-   illustrations and documents without personally identifiable profiling, tracking cookies or data
-   resale.
- Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / cec.org): Documentary integration and live
-   consultation of official data and documents from the public register SEM-26-003 hosted on the
-   institutional servers of the CCE. The loading or consultation of these documents and feeds is
-   carried out directly with the CCE infrastructure without intermediate processing or retention of
-   personal data by the site publisher.
- William Guindon uses the official custom domain hosted on the platform (Short.cm Inc.) for technical
-   link shortening purposes:
- Legal Obligation: William Guindon will only disclose personal information if expressly required to
-   do so by a valid court order, applicable law or court of competent jurisdiction, consistent with
-   the legal privileges and protections afforded to citizen sources, whistleblowers and public
-   interest correspondents.

## 8. Rights of Data Subjects (Loi 25, PIPEDA, GDPR)

Under the legislation applicable in Quebec and international data protection standards, you have the
following fundamental rights:
- Right of access: Right to request confirmation that personal information concerning you is held and
-   to obtain a clear and intelligible copy of it.
- Right of rectification: Right to demand the correction of any inaccurate, incomplete or ambiguous
-   personal information.
- Right to erasure (“right to be forgotten”): Right to demand the permanent deletion of your personal
-   information when its retention is no longer justified by law.
- Right to withdraw consent: Right to withdraw your consent to the processing of your data at any time
-   for the future.
- Right to portability: Right to receive your computerized personal information in a structured and
-   commonly used technological format.

### How to exercise your rights
To exercise any of these rights, you may send a written request to the Privacy Officer (William
Guindon) using the contact details below. A reasoned response will be provided to you free of charge
within a maximum period of thirty (30) days following receipt of your request, in accordance with
Loi 25.

If you believe that your rights have not been respected, you have the right to contact the competent
supervisory authority: the Commission d'access à l'information du Québec (CAI) (www.cai.gouv.qc.ca)
or the Office of the Privacy Commissioner of Canada (CPVP).

## 9. Data Security and Protection Measures

Rigorous physical, organizational and technological security measures are deployed to preserve the
integrity, confidentiality and availability of your data:
- Mandatory end-to-end TLS 1.3 / HTTPS encryption protocol with enhanced HSTS (HTTP Strict Transport
-   Security) preload policy;
- Delivery via Cloudflare secure edge network with application firewall (WAF) and active DDoS
-   protection;
- Restrictive Content Security Policy (CSP): whitelist of authorized domains, prohibition of embedded
-   objects (object-src 'none'), prohibition of external framing (frame-ancestors) and script-src
-   restricted to trusted domains. Dynamic code evaluation disabled on PDF reader side (
-   isEvalSupported: false );
- Protection against clickjacking via HTTP headers X-Frame-Options: DENY and frame-ancestors 'none'
-   served at the edge;
- Partitioning of hardware permissions (default deactivation of the camera, microphone, geolocation
-   and USB devices via Permissions-Policy);
- Zero Trust protection hermetically isolates administration interfaces without local storage of
-   master passwords.

## 10. Protection of Children and Minors

The site is a free access civic and scientific information platform. No personal information
relating to children or minors is knowingly collected without the required consent of those with
parental authority. If you become aware that a minor has transmitted personal data without
authorization, please notify us immediately for immediate deletion.

## 11. External Links

The site offers hyperlinks to third-party official platforms and registers (Commission de
coopération environnementale, ministries, media, scientific libraries). The publisher has no control
over the privacy policies of these third-party sites and declines all responsibility for their
respective practices.

## 12. Communication of Personal Information Outside of Quebec (Loi 25, art. 17)

In accordance with article 17 of the Act respecting the protection of personal information in the
private sector of Quebec (amended by Loi 25), William Guindon informs you that certain personal
information or technical metadata may be communicated, hosted or processed outside Quebec (notably
in the United States, Switzerland and the European Union) through its infrastructure providers and
specialized partners:
- United States: Cloudflare, Inc. (broadcast network, security and inference workers), GitHub Inc.
-   (hosting public static files), Cal.com, Inc. (appointment scheduling), Short.cm Inc. (technical
-   redirection of wg.s.gy short links), and Groq Inc. (transient inference of AI queries without
-   long-term retention).
- Switzerland: Infomaniak Network SA (SwissTransfer platform for the optional exchange of large
-   files).
- European Union: Umami Cloud / hosting of anonymized cookieless statistics.

Privacy Impact Assessment (PIA): In accordance with the strict requirements of section 17 of the Loi
25, a privacy impact assessment was conducted prior to the communication of information outside
Quebec. This evaluation concluded that the information benefits from a level of protection
equivalent to the principles enshrined in Quebec legislation, having regard to:
- The very limited sensitivity of the information processed (public browsing, ephemeral network
-   metrics, voluntary correspondence);
- For limited and proportionate technical purposes (anti-DDoS security, rapid web distribution,
-   document integrity);
- Contractual guarantees and proven cryptographic security measures deployed by service providers
-   (end-to-end TLS 1.3 encryption, SOC 2 / ISO 27001 certifications, no commercial profiling policy);
- To the legal protection framework of the jurisdictions concerned.

## 13. Modification of the Policy

This Privacy Policy may be modified periodically to reflect legal, jurisprudential or technological
developments. Any changes will be effective immediately upon posting online with an updated revision
date.

## 14. Contact the Data Protection Officer

For any questions, requests for clarification or exercise of your legal rights:

Title : Protection of the Documentary Archive — William Guindon

# Published Documentary Archive Protection Policy
Evidentiary fixity, copyright compliance, traceability and documented redaction and removal
procedure

### Preamble
WHEREAS the publication, on September 25, 2026, of the Documentary Archive of the file SEM-26-003 on
the Internet Archive service under the identifier
“dossier-journalistes-tourbiere-blainville-stablex”, as well as its linking on the “Dossier for
journalists” page of the platform;

WHEREAS the evidentiary integrity of the official ZIP archive is sealed by the cryptographic
fingerprint certified by OpenTimestamps (Blockchain Bitcoin): SHA256:
3209f9ea95dfc096ebcdf1ac0288c97872206af11a6e978a74761c03b3eba4d7;

WHEREAS the Author, a minor citizen, intends to protect himself, as well as third parties appearing
in the Archive, against the risks of invasion of privacy, harassment, doxxing, usurpation and
strategic lawsuits against public participation;

WHEREAS an independent audit of the Archive was conducted on September 26, 2026, the full text
extraction of which derived files revealed no leaks of personal contact information, telephone
numbers or postal codes;

WHEREAS the Author voluntarily chooses the fixity of the Archive, it remaining immutable by
evidential principle, the corrections taking place exclusively by the documented procedure of
challenge and withdrawal, recorded in a confidential internal audit log, never in silence;

WHEREAS certain documents published prior to this policy were not subject to the “BIFFÉ_AUDIT”
naming convention, without this affecting their protection;

CONSEQUENTLY, the following is adopted:

## Chapter I — General provisions

### Article 1 — Purpose
1.1. This policy establishes the protection measures applicable to the public documentary archive of
the file SEM-26-003, distributed in particular on the Internet Archive (archive.org), on the
“Dossier for journalists” page, in the ZIP archive and in the search engine.

1.2. It determines:
- a) the principle of fixity of the Archive and its documented exceptions;
- b) retroactive coverage of all published pieces, regardless of their naming convention;
- (c) copyright compliance rules, including those applicable to foreign hosting services;
- (d) the procedure for reporting, redaction and removal for the benefit of any person concerned;
- e) measures to protect privacy, in particular that of the minor Author.

### Article 2 — Definitions
For the purposes of this policy, unless the context indicates a different meaning, we mean:
- a) “Archive”: the Internet Archive item “file-journalists-tourbiere-blainville-stablex”, its
-   content, the ZIP archive, the search engine and the “File for journalists” page, together;
- b) “Fixity”: the principle according to which the Archive is only modified, reorganized or corrected
-   by a formal measure documented in the internal audit log, never silently;
- c) “Naming convention”: the affixing of the suffix “_BIFFE_AUDIT” to the published files, for
-   information only and not constituting protection;
- d) “Derivative”: files automatically generated by a hosting service from the pieces (text extracts,
-   thumbnails, feeds), which are subject to this policy in the same way as the original pieces;
- e) “Official Channel”: the email (contact@williamguindon.me) and encrypted channels indicated on
-   williamguindon.me, including the address appearing in the Archive's metadata, expressly designated
-   as the official contact channel for any report, request for redaction or withdrawal;
- f) “DMCA”: the Digital Millennium Copyright Act, the United States law governing copyright
-   infringement takedown requests made to hosting services;
- g) “Redaction Register”: the internal legal audit log, cryptographically sealed and strictly
-   confidential (non-public to avoid any re-exposure of sensitive data), maintained by the Author for
-   the purposes of forensic traceability and evidentiary compliance.

### Article 3 — Scope and retroactive coverage
3.1. This policy applies to any Item making up the Archive, regardless of the date, place or form of
its publication, including Derivatives.

3.2. Any Document published prior to the entry into force of this policy is deemed to be covered by
all of the protections and procedures it establishes.

3.3. The absence of the Naming Convention on a Part does not imply a waiver, weakening or exclusion
of the applicable protections; it has no effect on the rights of any person concerned.

## Chapter II — On the Fixity of the Archive

### Article 4 — Principle of fixity
4.1. The Archive is published as is and remains, by evidentiary principle, stable in its content:
Fixity preserves the value of the Chain of Custody, the cryptographic fingerprints and the
documentary history.

4.2. No file modifications, deletions or substitutions are made silently. All measurements are
time-stamped, justified and recorded in the Redactions Register (confidential internal audit log),
in accordance with traceability requirements.

4.3. Fixity does not prevent the withdrawal measures provided for in Chapter IV; it only guarantees
transparency and traceability.

### Article 5 — Corrections
5.1. Any correction of material errors (corrupt file, duplicate, naming error) is carried out
without altering the evidentiary content and is recorded in the Internal Register.

5.2. The replacement of a Part by a more complete redacted version constitutes a protective measure
within the meaning of Chapter IV, not a silent modification.

## Chapter III — Copyright, public interest and compliance

### Article 6 — Fair use, public interest and legal provisions authorizing dissemination
6.1. The constitution, publication and making available of the Documentary Archive are based on the
legal exceptions and fair use rights provided for by applicable national and international
legislation for the purposes of public interest, citizen research, education, criticism, reporting
and democratic participation:
- Copyright Act (Canada, R.S.C. 1985, c. C-42): Sections 29 (private study, research, education), 29.1
-   (criticism and reporting) and 29.2 (communication of news) guarantee the lawfulness of the
-   citation, analysis and reproduction of documents necessary for citizen evaluation. According to
-   the principled jurisprudence of the Supreme Court of Canada (CCH Canadienne Ltée v. Law Society of
-   Upper Canada, 2004 SCC 13), fair dealing constitutes a user right that is an integral part of
-   copyright and must receive a liberal interpretation in the service of the public interest;
- American law and hosting (17 U.S. Code § 107 — Fair Use): Non-commercial archiving on the Internet
-   Archive is fully within the framework of the fair use doctrine authorizing reproduction for the
-   purposes of criticism, comment, news coverage, scientific research and documentary preservation of
-   general interest;
- Transparency of administrative and environmental documents: Supporting documents resulting from
-   public hearing processes (BAPE 371), impact studies, administrative correspondence or filed as
-   part of public environmental procedures (Commission de coopération environnementale CCE
-   (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) / ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement), art.
-   24.27 and 24.28) fall under the public communication of acts of general interest, in accordance
-   with the applicable decrees on the reproduction of public documents (in particular the federal
-   decree P.C. 1996-1995) and the right to environmental information.

6.2. No third party works contained in the Archive are offered, licensed or sublicensed, in whole or
in part, under any license. Only the Author's original creations are subject to the CC BY-NC-ND 4.0
license.

6.3. In the event of a discrepancy between a metadata field of a third-party hosting service and the
Platform's Terms of Use, the latter alone express the will of the Author, who has never intended to
authorize commercial use of his creations nor to grant the slightest license to third-party works.

### Section 7 — DMCA Compliance and Third-Party Service Procedures
7.1. The Author fully subscribes to the DMCA takedown procedures applicable to hosting services in
the United States, including Internet Archive.

7.2. The Official Channel designated in Article 2 e) (contact@williamguindon.me) serves as the
notification address for any request for withdrawal or modification sent under copyright law; the
Author responds within a maximum of five (5) to ten (10) working days, taking into account the
legitimate requirements of his school and academic calendar.

7.3. The Author reserves all rights that the DMCA and Canadian law confer on him, including the
filing of a counter-notice if applicable.

## Chapter IV — Protection procedure

### Article 8 — Reporting
8.1. Any person - Rights holder, Private person, organization, close to a person appearing in the
Archive - can report, through the Official Channel (contact@williamguindon.me), any element of the
Archive that they consider to infringe their rights or their private life.

8.2. No prior proof of the existence of a risk is required from the reporter in good faith; the
precautionary principle applies.

8.3. No retaliatory action will be taken against a report made in good faith.

### Article 9 — Deadlines and measures
9.1. Upon receipt of a report, the Author acknowledges receipt in writing within a maximum of five
(5) working days. This period can be reasonably arranged during periods of official school exams or
academic holidays.

9.2. The Author will evaluate the report and take the appropriate action within a maximum period of
fifteen (15) working days following acknowledgment of receipt.

9.3. The default measure is the targeted redaction of the passage or Exhibit concerned,
supplemented, where appropriate, by the implementation of the redacted version of the Exhibit.

9.4. Upon express written request, the measure is the complete removal of the Piece, including the
third-party hosting service; the Author then immediately takes the steps specific to the service
concerned.

9.5. Any measure is entered in the Redaction Register (confidential internal journal not distributed
in order to protect the sensitive data withdrawn), with its date, its legal reason and the reference
of the Document, without ever recording the confidential value withdrawn.

## Chapter V — Protection of persons

### Article 10 — Privacy
10.1. Any private person may request the deletion of their personal data, their face, their contact
details or any element allowing them to be identified or located, without having to justify any
damage.

10.2. Requests relating to minors and vulnerable people are processed with absolute priority.

10.3. The personal information protection rules of Charter 10 apply to the Archive, including
Derivatives.

### Article 11 — Reinforced protection clause for the minor author and legal sanctuary
11.1. Best interests of the child and international protection: The Author acts as a minor citizen
committed to the defense of the environment, public health and future generations. In accordance
with Articles 3, 12, 13 and 16 of the United Nations Convention on the Rights of the Child (CRC) and
UN General Assembly Resolution 76/300, any civic and documentary participation of the Author is
protected against any form of retaliation, intimidation, harassment or unlawful attack on his honor
and reputation.

11.2. School, family and personal sanctuary: The private, family, residential and school sphere of
the Author is strictly inviolable (Code civil du Québec, arts. 3 and 35; Charter of human rights and
freedoms, RLRQ c. C-12, arts. 4 and 5; Charter 10). No formal notice, summons, communication or
physical or telephone approach may be sent to the educational establishments attended by the Author,
to his teachers, guardians, parents or relatives. Any legal contact must be made through the Single
Official Electronic Channel (contact@williamguindon.me).

11.3. Anti-SLAPP shield and sanctions for abuse of procedure: In accordance with articles 51 to 54
of the Code of Civil Procedure of Quebec (RLRQ c. C-25.01) and Charter 04, any attempted legal
action, threat of litigation, abusive formal notice or intimidation maneuver initiated with the aim
of restricting the Author's freedom of expression, draining his resources or hindering his citizen
participation in the case SEM-26-003 will be immediately reported to the courts as a Strategic
Lawsuit Against Public Participation (SLAPP) and a gross abuse of process. The Author will
immediately request the summary dismissal of the action, the imposition of a provision for costs,
the joint ordering of the plaintiffs and their representatives to pay punitive and exemplary
damages, as well as the ethical reporting to the competent professional orders.

11.4. Criminal protection against doxxing and harassment: Any attempt at unauthorized disclosure of
personal information (doxxing), shadowing, malicious surveillance, cyberintimidation or direct or
indirect threat aimed at the Author or his family circle will be subject to immediate priority
reporting to the police services (Sûreté du Québec / competent police force) and filing of
complaints under articles 264 (Criminal harassment), 372 (Harassing communications) and 423
(Intimidation) of the Criminal Code of Canada.

11.5. Conventional international law and environmental immunity: The filing and monitoring of the
citizen communication SEM-26-003 with the Secretariat of the Commission de coopération
environnementale (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) is the exercise of a formal right
guaranteed by articles 24.27 and 24.28 of the Canada-United States-Mexico Agreement (ACEUM). No
direct or indirect retaliatory measure can be tolerated against the constitution and evidentiary
safeguarding of this international public file.

11.6. Absolute good faith, gratuity and absence of intent to harm: The Author acts on a strictly
voluntary, independent and self-financed basis, without any profit or commercial interest. The
placing of the Archive online responds exclusively to evidentiary reasons of general interest
(contamination of soil and water with cadmium, Loi 93 (Bill 93), protection of Grande Tourbière de
Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog)). The establishment of an amicable, transparent and free
redaction and withdrawal procedure confirms the uninterrupted good faith of the Author and excludes
any fault of intent or culpable negligence.

## Chapter VI — Final provisions

### Article 12 — Transparency and audit
12.1. An audit of the Archive was carried out on September 26, 2026, the main findings of which —
the absence of leakage of personal contact details in the textual extraction of the Derivatives —
can be communicated to any journalist or researcher who requests it through the Official Channel.

12.2. The Author may have any additional audit carried out; the findings are recorded and made
available under the same conditions.

### Article 13 — Limits
13.1. This policy is a citizen self-regulation policy; it does not constitute legal advice and does
not prejudge the outcome of a possible dispute.

13.2. It does not create any obligations for third parties and does not restrict any rights
conferred on them by law.

### Article 14 — Entry into force, revision and adoption
14.1. This policy comes into force on the date of its publication. It is anchored by OpenTimestamps
timestamp in accordance with Charter 07.

14.2. It complements, without replacing them, Charter 11 (Publication of annexes and respect for
copyright), Charter 10 (Privacy), Charter 08 (OpSec) and Charter 04 (Anti-SLAPP), with which it
reads as a whole.

14.3. The register of platform charters increases from eleven (11) to twelve (12) fundamental
charters.

14.4. Any revision is the subject of a numbered, dated and kept version.

PAGE 44/50 · https://williamguindon.me/stablex.html
Title : Stablex Blainville file: Cell 6, BAPE 371, Loi 93 & Appeal CCE SEM-26-003
==============================================================================

## 1. What is Stablex?

Located at 760 boulevard Industriel in Blainville, the Stablex industrial installation was
commissioned in 1983, at a time when Quebec environmental standards and hydrological knowledge of
peat wetlands were embryonic. The plant mainly receives liquid, pasty and solid inorganic hazardous
materials: metal surface treatment sludge, mining residues, incineration ashes, contaminated soil
and industrial waste with a high concentration of heavy metals (cadmium, lead, nickel, copper,
arsenic, chromium).

The operating principle is based on an alkaline reaction: liquid or muddy waste is neutralized,
mixed with chemical additives, then amalgamated with binding agents based on lime and Portland
cement. The resulting paste, commercially called “Stablex,” is pumped and dumped into gigantic
landfill basins called cells. As the mixture hardens, it forms a matrix that is supposed to
chemically and physically trap pollutants to prevent them from leaching into ground and surface
water.

However, scientific and citizen criticisms formulated over four decades of exploitation point to the
long-term geochemical degradation of this material under the action of the natural acidity of the
peatlands (acid pH between 3.5 and 4.5), northern freeze-thaw cycles and rainwater infiltration,
calling into question the perpetual impermeability promised by the developer.

## 2. Who owns Stablex? Ownership structure and multinationals

Contrary to a widespread perception that Stablex is a local Quebec company, its share capital and
decision-making power are entirely controlled from abroad. After belonging to the British Hanson
group, then to the American Waste Services group, Stablex was integrated into US Ecology Inc. In May
2022, Republic Services Inc., listed on the New York Stock Exchange and with a market capitalization
of several tens of billions of dollars, completed the complete acquisition of US Ecology.

This membership of a leading American conglomerate sheds light on a fundamental aspect of the
economic model of the Blainville site: the massive importation of American hazardous waste. Under
the Canada-United States Agreement Concerning Transboundary Movements of Hazardous Wastes (1986) and
the Basel Convention, tens of thousands of tonnes of highly toxic waste from plants in New England,
New York and the US Midwest cross the border each year to be stabilized and buried in perpetuity in
Blainville.

According to the briefs submitted during the public hearings, more than 50% of certain categories of
treated waste come directly from the United States, transforming the municipality of Blainville and
the Grande Tourbière (Great Peat Bog) into a receptacle for cross-border industrial hazardous
materials.

## 3. Cell No. 6: The expansion project in Grande Tourbière

### What do 278,000 m² of threatened peatland represent in concrete terms?
The encroachment of Cell No. 6 is equivalent to razing a continuous surface of 183 ice rinks at the
Bell Center in the heart of a thousand-year-old, non-replaceable ombrotrophic bog.

Since 1983, Stablex has operated and successively filled five landfill cells (cells 1, 2, 3, 4 and
5). Faced with the foreseeable exhaustion of the residual capacity of cell no. 5 by 2025-2026, the
company has submitted a vast expansion project: cell no. 6.

This project involves the development of a gigantic new pit of 27.8 hectares to the southeast of the
existing facilities. The major problem lies in its geographical base: the targeted land is entirely
composed of ombrotrophic peat bogs (bogs) and mature wooded wetlands forming the nerve center of the
Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat Bog), one of the last large intact peat
complexes in the Montreal metropolitan region.

The construction of cell no. 6 involves drainage, felling trees, excavating several meters of
organic matter accumulated over 8,000 years and installing a geosynthetic waterproofing membrane.
This mechanical destruction wipes out a major carbon sink capable of sequestering thousands of tons
of CO₂ and destroys the vital habitat of wildlife and plant species designated as threatened or
vulnerable, such as the milk snake, monarch butterfly, snapping turtle and western chorus frog.

[Caption] Official map of the Ville de Blainville: The 550.0 hectares of protected municipal natural
environments (in orange/gold) and the 55.8 hectares acquired via the Green and Blue Network (in
green) directly surround the basins and the industrial footprint of Stablex. Open in high definition
↗

## 4. The report BAPE 371: The unfavorable opinion of the State experts

Following the public hearings held in fall 2020, the BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental
Hearings) commission of inquiry analyzed more than a hundred briefs from experts, citizens,
environmental organizations (Centre québécois du droit de l'environnement - CQDE, SNAP Québec
(Society for Nature and Parks) - SNAP Québec) and municipal representatives.

Official report no. 371, submitted to the Quebec Minister of the Environment, formulated a
resounding disavowal for the promoter:
- Irreplaceable ecological value: The Grande Tourbière de Blainville is recognized as an ecosystem of
-   exceptional interest whose hydrological, microclimatic and natural water filtering functions
-   cannot be artificially recreated through financial compensation.
- Lack of alternative location efforts: The BAPE severely criticized the company for not having looked
-   for degraded industrial sites or anthropized land outside the peatland zone to establish its new
-   cell.
- Incompatibility of use: The permanent burial of dangerous materials in an environment saturated with
-   water and linked to the regional water table presents disproportionate hydrological risks.

Based on these clear conclusions, Ville de Blainville (City of Blainville), owner of the targeted
forest lots, formally refused to transfer its land to Stablex and adopted a strict policy of
conservation of its natural heritage.

## 5. The Loi 93: Forced expropriation by exceptional legislative decree

Faced with the categorical refusal of the Ville de Blainville to sell its land and the regulatory
blockage resulting from the BAPE 371 report, the government of Quebec chose to bypass the common law
of land use planning by filing the Projet de loi 93 (entitled An Act concerning the project to
expand the Stablex Canada Inc. hazardous materials landfill in Blainville).

This direct political intervention sparked a democratic shock wave across the province:
- Forced expropriation of a municipality: The law orders the transfer of municipal property titles to
-   the private company without the agreement of the City, creating an alarming precedent for the
-   autonomy of Quebec's 1,100 municipalities.
- Adoption under gag order: The parliamentary debates were brutally interrupted by the executive to
-   force the text to be adopted on March 28, 2025, ignoring the protests of the opposition parties,
-   the Union des municipalités du Québec (UMQ) and environmental law lawyers.
- Judicialization of the conflict: The Ville de Blainville immediately challenged the constitutional
-   validity of the law before the courts, denouncing an abuse of legislative power motivated by the
-   corporate interests of an American multinational.

To deepen the legal and political analysis:

Consult our dedicated satellite file: The Loi 93 (Bill 93) decrypted: Legislative chronology, forced
expropriation and legal battle ↗

## 6. Water contamination analyzes and company position

The question of leachate leaks and the migration of contaminants out of the historic Stablex cells
constitutes the most burning scientific element of the file. While the developer touts the
durability of its solidification process, surface and groundwater samples have documented serious
chemical anomalies:
  ▸ Parameter analyzed
  ▸ Aquatic life standard (chronic)
  ▸ Measurement observed downstream (Dec. 2023)
  ▸ Overrun ratio
  · Cadmium (Cd)
  · 0.00025 mg/L
  · 0.080 mg/L
  · 320× standard
  · Zinc (Zn)
  · 0.030 mg/L
  · 0.420 mg/L
  · 14× the standard
  · Sulfates (SO₄)
  · 128 mg/L
  · 760 mg/L
  · 6× the norm

The independent hydrogeological study carried out in June 2026 by the specialist firm WaterShed
Monitoring highlighted the vulnerability of the local hydrographic network. The surface water of the
sector flows towards the tributary network of the Mille Îles River and crosses the redeveloped
gravel pits of Fauvel Lakes, a municipal recreational-ecological park very frequented by the
citizens of Blainville.

The official position of Stablex Canada Inc. : The company categorically rejects responsibility for
these excesses. In its briefs and public statements, Stablex states:
- That its facilities operate in strict compliance with the Regulation on the landfilling and
-   incineration of dangerous materials (REIMD) and its environmental authorization certificate issued
-   by the MELCCFP (Québec Ministry of the Environment, the Fight against Climate Change, Wildlife and
-   Parks);
- That its network of regulatory monitoring piezometers does not record any indication of membrane
-   breakage or significant percolation;
- That the heavy metals measured in the peripheral ditches could come from the regional industrial
-   past (old Plan Bouchard embankments) or from the natural geological background of local sediments.

This fundamental divergence between independent data and industry declarations is precisely the
heart of the reasons which justified the referral to international authorities.

To view full sample data and charts:

Consult our satellite file: Cadmium contamination and hydrogeology of Fauvel lakes: Raw data and
scientific analysis ↗

## 7. The international appeal CCE SEM-26-003 before the ACEUM

Faced with the Quebec political impasse created by Loi 93, an international citizen legal recourse
was initiated under Chapter 24 (Environment) of the Canada-United States-Mexico Agreement (ACEUM).
William Guindon, a 15-year-old citizen of Blainville, submitted an official documented submission of
more than 200 pages and supported by 222 public evidentiary documents.

The communication cites the failure to apply two fundamental laws of the Canadian Parliament:
- The Fisheries Act (L.R.C. 1985, c. F-14): In particular sections 34.4, 35 and 36(3), which formally
-   prohibit the deposit of harmful substances in waters where fish live or in a place where these
-   substances risk entering these waters. The hydrological connections between the peatland and the
-   fish-bearing waterways are at the center of the complaint.
- Endangered Species Act (SARA, S.C. 2002, c. 29): The Cell No. 6 project encroaches on documented
-   critical habitat of threatened species without a valid federal environmental impact permit.

On August 17, 2026, the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) Secretariat made its official
determination under section 24.27(2) and (3) of the ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement),
concluding that the citizen communication meets all strict legal criteria and requiring the
Government of Canada to provide a formal written response by October 16, 2026. William Guindon on
this occasion became the first minor author in the history of the SEM mechanism since its
establishment in 1994.

The ultimate challenge of this procedure is the development of an independent international factual
file publicly exposing in the eyes of the three member countries of the ACEUM the environmental
protection flaws surrounding the Stablex file.

Consult the Official Register CCE SEM-26-003 ↗ Explore the 222 evidentiary documents ↗

## 8. Frequently asked questions (FAQ) about the Stablex file

### What is Stablex and where is the factory located?
Stablex Canada Inc. is an industrial hazardous waste treatment and landfill plant located at 760
boulevard Industriel in Blainville, Quebec. Active since 1983, it neutralizes and solidifies
inorganic chemical sludge by adding lime and cement before burying it in waterproofed pits adjacent
to the Grande Tourbière de Blainville.

### Who owns the Stablex company?
The company is a 100% subsidiary of the American group US Ecology, itself purchased in May 2022 by
the giant Republic Services (NYSE: RSG) for 2.2 billion USD. More than 50% of hazardous materials
processed on site are imported from the United States via the rail and road network.

### Why is the Cell No. 6 project so contested?
Cell No. 6 provides for the burial of 1.7 million tonnes of stabilized waste over 278,000 m² (27.8
hectares) directly excavated in the Grande Tourbière de Blainville. This project destroys an
irreplaceable ombrotrophic wetland and the habitat of threatened species, in contradiction with the
expert opinion of BAPE 371.

### What is the Loi 93 adopted on March 28, 2025?
The Loi 93 is an exceptional law adopted under gag order by the Assemblée nationale du Québec
(National Assembly of Québec). She orders the forced expropriation of land belonging to Ville de
Blainville to hand it over to Stablex, thus circumventing the democratic refusal of the municipal
council and the unfavorable opinion of BAPE.

### What are the contamination levels measured around the site?
Independent sampling from December 2023 revealed cadmium concentrations up to 320 times higher than
provincial criteria for the protection of aquatic life in drainage ditches, corroborated by the
WaterShed Monitoring study from June 2026. Stablex disputes any leak and claims to respect its
ministerial authorizations.

### What is the scope of the SEM-26-003 appeal to CCE?
The citizen communication SEM-26-003 filed by William Guindon before the Commission de coopération
environnementale (CCE / ACEUM) aims to establish the failure to apply the Fisheries Act and the
Species at Risk Act by Canada. The Canadian government must submit its official response by October
16, 2026.

# Terms of use
Last legal update in effect: September 6, 2026 (September 6, 2026)

### Purpose of the Service and Public Interest Framework
These General Conditions of Use govern access, consultation and use of the site williamguindon.me.
This site is an independent citizen portal for information, scientific documentation and legal
popularization dedicated to monitoring the international citizen communication SEM-26-003 filed
before the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United States–Mexico
Agreement)), relating to the protection of the Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great Blainville Peat
Bog) and the management of hazardous materials in Quebec.

## 1. Acceptance of Conditions and Legal Capacity

By accessing this website, browsing its pages or using its tools (in particular secure messaging or
the AI ​​search assistant), you acknowledge having read these Conditions of Use as well as the
Privacy Policy, and agree to be bound by them without reservation.

If you do not agree to all of these stipulations, you must immediately cease all use of the site.

## 2. Independence and Lack of Official Governmental Status

Formal disclaimer: This website is an independent citizen initiative edited by William Guindon,
submitter of communication SEM-26-003. It is in no way the official portal of the Commission de
coopération environnementale (CCE), the Secretariat of the Canada–United States–Mexico Agreement
(ACEUM), the Government of Canada, the Government of Quebec or the Ville de Blainville (City of
Blainville).

Official documents from the CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation) public register are
reproduced or linked for the purposes of public information, democratic transparency and citizen
research.

## 3. Protection of Public Interest Debate and Anti-SLAPP Clause (Anti-SLAPP)

All publications, field surveys, documentary analyses, photographs, extracts from registers and
positions published on this site fall strictly within the framework of the legitimate exercise of
freedom of expression and freedom of the press protected by:
- Article 3 of the Quebec Charter of Human Rights and Freedoms (RLRQ, c. C-12);
- Section 2 b) of the Canadian Charter of Rights and Freedoms;
- Articles 51 to 54 of the Code of Civil Procedure of Quebec (RLRQ, c. C-25.01) severely punish
-   abusive prosecutions, judicial intimidation procedures and strategic prosecutions against public
-   mobilization (SLAPP suits or SLAPPs).

Any attempt at judicial intimidation or proceedings aimed at obstructing the communication of
verified information of public interest on environmental health and international registries will be
vigorously contested and will expose its perpetrators to the sanctions and damages provided for by
law.

Title : Traceability of Digital Evidence — William Guindon

# Chain of Custody Protocol for Digital Evidence
OpenTimestamps decentralized timestamping, cryptographic integrity and evidentiary preservation

## Cryptographic integrity checker SHA-256 (100% local)

Verify in one click or drag a downloaded PDF file to locally calculate its SHA-256 footprint via the
crypto.subtle.digest native API and check its compliance with the deposited documents register
(SEM-26-003):

## 1. Objective and transversal scope

This protocol documents the method by which William Guindon and his site guarantee the provenance,
authenticity and cryptographic integrity of all probative elements (official documents, field data,
water and soil analyses, screenshots, correspondence, CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation)
and UN depositions), in order to be able to demonstrate, before any jurisdiction or international
body, that a part has not undergone any modification and existed on a certified date.

A rigorous evidentiary “chain of custody” is based on three inseparable pillars: authenticity
(identified source of origin), integrity (absence of alteration), and traceability (complete history
of technical manipulations and transfers).

## 2. General principles

- Keep the raw original and work exclusively on copies.
- Minimize handling; document each technical manipulation.
- Reliably and independently timestamp each important element.
- Secure storage (controlled access) and maintain redundant backups.
- Document the collection method (source, date, device, context).

## 3. Using OpenTimestamps (decentralized timestamping)

The site notably uses OpenTimestamps to prove, independently and verifiable by a third party, that a
file existed in a given state at a specific time:
- We calculate the cryptographic fingerprint (hash SHA-256) of the original file;
- This fingerprint is anchored in the Bitcoin blockchain by OpenTimestamps, which produces a receipt
-   (.ots);
- The .ots receipt and the original file are kept together;
- At any time, a third party or a court can verify for free that the file has not changed.

Honest Transparency: OpenTimestamps proves the existence and integrity of a file on a date, but does
not alone prove the veracity of its contents. It adds to the context documentation.

## 4. Email archiving and internal registry

William's professional emails are archived in their entirety (headers, body, attachments). The email
measurement tool (mail tracker) only reports an approximate number of opens and does not collect any
personally identifying data. A dated internal register records identifiers, hashes, dates and
transfer histories.

## 5. Secure storage

Sensitive elements are kept in a secure storage space with controlled access (William and his
parents), with regular backups. Keys and identifiers are never transmitted to a third party or to an
AI model.

This protocol is an independent policy of williamguindon.me. Operational details remain internal. No
signature is required.

# William Guindon — Official Site
Quebec environmental activist (15 years) · File CCE (Commission for Environmental Cooperation)
SEM-26-003 Blainville, Quebec, Canada

## 1. Summary of file CCE / ACEUM (SEM-26-003)

On August 17, 2026, the Commission de coopération environnementale (CCE / ACEUM (Canada–United
States–Mexico Agreement)) Secretariat issued a historic affirmative determination (26-2-DET2)
requiring a response from the Government of Canada regarding the non-enforcement of its federal
environmental laws in the face of Stablex's expansion into Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Great
Blainville Peat Bog).

On October 16, 2026, Canada filed its 59-page official response (72-RSP), requesting the procedural
closure of the file by invoking the municipal judicial review of Blainville and federal
environmental petition No. 0546 (BVG/CEDD). As of October 17, 2026, William Guindon (15 years old,
first minor in the history of the SEM process since 1994 to complete these stages) sent his brief of
observations demonstrating that no court is examining federal inaction and requesting the creation
of a factual file (art. 24.28 ACEUM — deadline of 60 working days).

Next deadlines: November 10, 2026 (mandatory ministerial response to BVG petition no. 0546) and
parliamentary meetings in Ottawa in November 2026 with Nadine Le Gal (Office of the leader of the
Bloc Québécois) and Senator Madeleine Chenette.

## 2. Essential facts & key figures

- Site: Grande Tourbière de Blainville (Laurentides, Quebec) — 278,000 m² of threatened wetlands.
- BAPE (Québec Bureau of Public Environmental Hearings): Report 371 (September 2023) concluding that
-   the cell 6 project is “premature” with recommendation of refusal.
- Loi 93 (Bill 93): Special law adopted under gag order on March 28, 2025 by Assemblée nationale du
-   Québec (National Assembly of Québec) to circumvent municipal opposition and restrict legal
-   recourse.
- Contamination: Analyzes revealing cadmium levels up to 320 times the protection thresholds for
-   aquatic life (surface water) and 31.8× to 57.6× in sediments.
- UN: Formal testimony transmitted in May 2026 to the UN Special Rapporteur on toxic substances and
-   human rights (Dr. Marcos A. Orellana) and statement of support from Manfred Liebel (E-7834).

## 3. Official documents (Direct download)

- Memorandum of citizen observations on Canada's response — October 17, 2026 (PDF)
- Official Canadian Response (72-RSP) — October 16, 2026 (PDF)
- Acknowledgment of receipt from the Secretariat CCE (73-REC, art. 24.28) — October 16, 2026 (PDF)
- Positive determination of the Secretariat CCE — August 17, 2026 (PDF)
- Revised communication SEM-26-003 — July 16, 2026 (PDF)
- Federal Environmental Petition No. 0546 (BVG / CEDD) — Deadline Nov. 10, 2026 (PDF)
- Official press releases
- Public Appearances & Investigative Reporting
- Complete text corpus for artificial intelligence (llms-full.txt)

## 4. Transcriptions & Verbatim of video reports

Standalone raw file for AI models and light readers: transcriptions.txt (certified text format,
without journalistic questions).

### 📺 William Guindon — TVBL studio interview (July 2026)
- Citizenship, Loi 93 and legal recourse: “The results of this are made to affirm the truth, provide
-   evidence, then have a follow-up. But with this law, we cannot give our citizen point of view. At
-   the UN, it is the right of every person to a healthy, long-term environment for future
-   generations. According to the Charter of Rights and Freedoms, any human can legally express their
-   point of view. In my opinion, it is not normal that it is impossible in Quebec to have legal
-   recourse. »
- Operation of the SEM citizen mechanism and federal scope: “For the SEM, basically, it is a process
-   where all citizens and also organizations can undertake an environmental approach when, in their
-   opinion, federal law is not respected. So it doesn't apply to a plant as is, nor to the provincial
-   government, but really to the federal government. It is the federal government that must properly
-   apply its laws. This exchange really allows citizens to tell the truth about what is happening,
-   and then we can choose whether it is confidential or not. In my opinion, it allows us to tell the
-   truth to the world and show that Canada is not perfect. »
- Citizen role and responsibility for legal investigation: “In quotes, I do the work that the ministry
-   should do. In my opinion, it is not normal that it becomes a workload for me, when the government
-   has thousands of people who are paid $250 an hour to cite articles of law. I do it for several
-   articles. Then, I have to go deeper to take the steps and provide proof, of course. Then the most
-   difficult point is that I have to prove the legal recourses that fall to the federal government.
-   It’s some in-depth work that needs to be done to prove the truth. Whether the factory complies or
-   not, I just want the truth, that's my goal. »
- Approaches to the Federal Ministry of the Environment: “At the Federal Ministry of the Environment,
-   unfortunately, I have had no response. It's a bit disappointing on their part. A person wrote to
-   me on June 28 to say: “Hello William, we will send this to the minister's office, it is at their
-   discretion to answer your questions. » But they must act, they must not wait for the file to come
-   out in the media to make a move. »
- Respect for laws, territory and transparency: “I am not asking that the factory close, because in my
-   opinion it is unrealistic, even if it could be possible to a certain extent. But the goal is not
-   to take endless territory for waste. Basically, I just want her to respect the laws and for us to
-   stop claiming that everything is correct. It is not normal to always tell citizens “everything is
-   perfect, everything is correct”. Give us proof, because without proof, we cannot believe you, and
-   citizens begin to lack confidence. »

### 🎬 William Guindon — Official video capsule & Social networks (October 2026)
- Alert on the October 16 deadline and quest for truth: “The Government of Canada has until October 16
-   to explain itself in an international investigation. My goal is just to get the truth. »
- Intergenerational equity and fish habitat (222 technical evidence): “If I understand correctly,
-   there were a lot of words in your question. The principle of intergenerational equity, quoting:
-   "it is important to meet the needs of the present generation without compromising the ability of
-   future generations to meet their own needs", is also federal. Concerning fish habitat, I
-   documented this inaction with 222 technical evidence and submitted the case to CCE, the official
-   arbiter responsible for enforcing environmental laws. »
- Historical determination and Ottawa's legal obligation: “The result is a historic determination. The
-   international secretariat judged my citizen survey. Ottawa is now legally obligated to respond by
-   October 16. The entire file is on my website. Share this video en masse to follow the rest. »

### 🎙️ William Guindon — CBC Podcasts Interview & City Council
- Statement of Commitment (Municipal Council): “I told myself we had to do something. »
- On the adoption of the Loi 93 and the citizen bypass (CBC): “We apply the Projet de loi 93 (Bill
-   93), we reject the calls, we do not listen to the citizens and we are simply moving forward
-   quickly with this expansion project. »

### 💧 Dr Sonja Behmel (Water Specialist and Researcher, WaterShed Monitoring)
- Contaminant Migration and Wetland Value: “There are areas where we are virtually certain that
-   contaminants are leaving the site, either by air, groundwater or surface water runoff. Wetlands
-   are our most valuable source of biodiversity. To make sure we have them when heavy rains bring
-   flooding risks, and for areas that put water back into the environment in times of drought, and
-   for ecosystems that filter out our contaminants, it's like we're destroying ourselves. »
- Citizen concerns and environmental governance: “I have the impression that the people of Blainville
-   are extremely worried and that they want answers. I think that beyond wanting answers, my feeling
-   is that they really want the government to comply with the mechanisms that it is supposed to put
-   in place. We know we need industry, we need places where we can dispose of our waste and
-   everything else, but it should be done according to the governance mechanisms, and we should
-   respect them at every step, because otherwise we will destroy our environment. »

### 🏛️ Jorge Daniel Taillant (outgoing Executive Director of CCE / ACEUM)
- Binding force of the treaty and integrity of the ACEUM: “It was a surprise, at first we said to
-   ourselves, well, maybe it's a mistake, maybe we shouldn't be on this list, and the more we look,
-   maybe it was a mistake. We are treaty based. We are part of ACEUM. One cannot withdraw or cease
-   participation in part of the treaty without undermining the entire treaty. »
- Tripartite funding context and transition: “We have not received new funds from the United States
-   for 2026, which has created problems because without funding the CCE cannot function. Canada and
-   Mexico, while remaining committed to CCE, have also suspended their financial support. We are in a
-   period of transition, awaiting clarification on how this will unfold. »
- Ecological emergency and power of citizen action: “The most important things are the challenges we
-   face today, the urgency of fighting climate change and the urgency of what is perhaps the sixth
-   extinction of biodiversity which is taking place before our eyes. People want to know, want to get
-   involved and have more say than ever in their environment. The survival of CCE is essential to our
-   cooperative work, but more importantly, public policies must be attentive to communities, and
-   communities will increasingly demand action. »

### ⚖️ Paolo Solano (Director of Legal Affairs of CCE)
- Operation and resources of the legal unit: “Last year we had a team of four people; a staff member
-   left. Structurally, there is a position for someone else, but in the current circumstances we are
-   not hiring more; It's a question of budget. »
